(\mbox{$\cal M$}_{WTV}^{*}\circ V)^{*}\ \wedge\ \mbox{$\cal M$}_{V}={\mathbf{0}}_{V}
and

(\mbox{$\cal M$}_{WTV}^{*}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\cal M$}_{V}^{*})^{*}\times T\ \wedge\ \mbox{$\cal M$}_{T}={\mathbf{0}}_{T},

i.e., (using Theorems
42
,
43
,
50
)
(\mbox{$\cal M$}_{WTV}\times TV)\ \wedge\ (\mbox{$\cal M$}_{T}\oplus\mbox{$\cal M$}_{V})={\mathbf{0}}_{TV},

iff

(\mbox{$\cal M$}_{WTV}\times V)\ \wedge\ \mbox{$\cal M$}_{V}={\mathbf{0}}_{V}
and

(\mbox{$\cal M$}_{WTV}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\cal M$}_{V})\times T\ \wedge\ \mbox{$\cal M$}_{T}={\mathbf{0}}_{T}.

Thus the zero intersection property holds for
\{\mbox{$\cal M$}_{WTV},(\mbox{$\cal M$}_{T}\oplus\mbox{$\cal M$}_{V})\}
iff it holds for
\{\mbox{$\cal M$}_{WTV},\mbox{$\cal M$}_{V}\}
and for

\{(\mbox{$\cal M$}_{WTV}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\cal M$}_{V}),\mbox{$\cal M$}_{T}\}.

2. We have
\mbox{$\cal M$}_{WTV}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\cal M$}_{V}:\equiv(\mbox{$\cal M$}_{WTV}\wedge\mbox{$\cal M$}_{V})\circ WT=(\mbox{$\cal M$}_{WTV}\vee\ \mbox{$\cal M$}_{V})\times WT
(using Theorem
47
).

Rigidity of
\{\mbox{$\cal M$}_{WTV}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\cal M$}_{V},\mbox{$\cal M$}_{T}\}

is equivalent to the full sum and zero intersection conditions

((\mbox{$\cal M$}_{WTV}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\cal M$}_{V})\circ T)\vee\mbox{$\cal M$}_{T}=\mbox{$\cal F$}_{T},

and
((\mbox{$\cal M$}_{WTV}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\cal M$}_{V})\times T)\wedge\mbox{$\cal M$}_{T}={\mathbf{0}}_{T}.

The full sum condition is equivalent to
((\mbox{$\cal M$}_{WTV}\wedge\mbox{$\cal M$}_{V})\circ WT\circ T)\vee\mbox{$\cal M$}_{T}=((\mbox{$\cal M$}_{WTV}\wedge\mbox{$\cal M$}_{V})\circ T)\vee\mbox{$\cal M$}_{T}=\mbox{$\cal F$}_{T}

and the zero intersection condition is equivalent to
((\mbox{$\cal M$}_{WTV}\vee\mbox{$\cal M$}_{V})\times WT\times T)\wedge\mbox{$\cal M$}_{T}

=((\mbox{$\cal M$}_{WTV}\vee\mbox{$\cal M$}_{V})\times T)\wedge\mbox{$\cal M$}_{T}={\mathbf{0}}_{T}.

3. Let us say matroid
\mbox{$\cal M$}^{1}_{X}\geq\mbox{$\cal M$}^{2}_{X},

iff every base of
\mbox{$\cal M$}^{1}_{X}
contains a base of
\mbox{$\cal M$}^{2}_{X},

and every base of
\mbox{$\cal M$}^{2}_{X},
is contained in a base of
\mbox{$\cal M$}^{1}_{X}.

We observe that

(\mbox{$\cal M$}_{WTV}\vee\mbox{$\cal M$}_{V})\geq(\mbox{$\cal M$}_{WTV}\wedge\mbox{$\cal M$}_{V}),
so that

(\mbox{$\cal M$}_{WTV}\vee\mbox{$\cal M$}_{V})\circ T\geq(\mbox{$\cal M$}_{WTV}\wedge\mbox{$\cal M$}_{V})\circ T
and
(\mbox{$\cal M$}_{WTV}\vee\mbox{$\cal M$}_{V})\times T\geq(\mbox{$\cal M$}_{WTV}\wedge\mbox{$\cal M$}_{V})\times T.

Therefore, if
\{\mbox{$\cal M$}_{WTV}\wedge\mbox{$\cal M$}_{V},\mbox{$\cal M$}_{T}\}
satisfies the full
sum condition, so does
\{\mbox{$\cal M$}_{WTV}\vee\mbox{$\cal M$}_{V},\mbox{$\cal M$}_{T}\}

and if

\{\mbox{$\cal M$}_{WTV}\vee\mbox{$\cal M$}_{V},\mbox{$\cal M$}_{T}\}
satisfies the zero
intersection condition, so does
\{\mbox{$\cal M$}_{WTV}\wedge\mbox{$\cal M$}_{V},\mbox{$\cal M$}_{T}\}.

The result now follows from the previous part.
∎

Appendix B
Matroidal conditions for rigidity of multiports

The matroidal approach to testing rigidity of multiports
is based on the following lemma
which speaks of the rigidity of
\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{AB},{\cal A}_{B}\}

where
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{AB}
is over
{\mathbb{Q}}
and
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{B}
is in terms of parameters which are algebraically
independent over
{\mathbb{Q}}.

Lemma 71
.

Let
{\cal A}^{2}_{B}:\equiv{\cal A}^{2}_{B_{1}B_{2}}
be the solution space of the equation

\displaystyle{y_{B_{2}}}={D}{x_{B_{1}}}+{s}.

(10)

where
D
is a diagonal matrix.
Let
{\cal A}^{2}_{B_{1}B_{2}}
have
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B_{1}B_{2}}
as its vector space translate.
Let the diagonal entries of
D
be algebraically independent over

{\mathbb{Q}}.

Let
(C_{B_{1}}|C_{B_{2}})
be the representative matrix of a vector space
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{B_{1}B_{2}}

with entries from
{\mathbb{Q}}.

Let
there be a set of columns
j_{1},j_{2},\cdots,j_{n}

of
(C_{B_{1}}|C_{B_{2}}),

which are linearly independent,
and whose complement with respect to
B_{1}\uplus B_{2},

say
i_{1},i_{2},\cdots,i_{m}
is a
column base of the matrix

\displaystyle F=\begin{pmatrix}F_{B_{1}}|F_{B_{2}}\end{pmatrix}:\equiv\begin{pmatrix}I&D\end{pmatrix}.

(11)

We then have the following.

1.

Let
r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{B_{1}B_{2}})+r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B_{1}B_{2}})\geq|B_{1}\uplus B_{2}|=|B|.

Then
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{B_{1}B_{2}},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B_{1}B_{2}}
have the full sum property.

2.

Let
r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{B_{1}B_{2}})+r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B_{1}B_{2}})\leq|B_{1}\uplus B_{2}|=|B|.

Then
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{B_{1}B_{2}},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B_{1}B_{2}}
have the zero intersection property.

3.

Let
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}
be a vector space over
{\mathbb{Q}}
and let
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}

be the vector space translate of
{\cal A}^{2}_{B}
defined by Equation
10
.

(a)

\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}+\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B})=\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB})\vee\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B})
and

\mbox{$\cal M$}((\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB})^{\perp}+(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B})^{\perp})=\mbox{$\cal M$}((\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB})^{\perp})\vee\mbox{$\cal M$}((\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B})^{\perp}).

(b)

The following are equivalent.

i.

There exists a pair of disjoint bases
b^{1},b^{2}
of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B},

respectively such that
b^{1}\cup b^{2}\supseteq B.

ii.

r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}\circ B)+r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B})\geq|B|

and
r((\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB})^{\perp}\circ B)+r((\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B})^{\perp})\geq|B|.

iii.

\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}\}
is rigid.

iv.

\{\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}),\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B})\}
is rigid.

Proof.

1. It is clear that
F
is a representative matrix
for
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}.

Let
(C_{2B_{1}}|C_{2B_{2}})

be a suitable submatrix of

(C_{B_{1}}|C_{B_{2}})

such that

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}C_{2B_{1}}&C_{2B_{2}}\\
I&D\end{pmatrix}

(12)

is square. This is possible since

r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{B_{1}B_{2}})+r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B_{1}B_{2}})\geq|B_{1}\uplus B_{2}|=|B|.

Perform a Laplace expansion of the determinant
of this matrix using the first set of rows
as one block in the partition of rows.
One term in this expansion is
\pm det(C_{2}^{j})\times det(F^{i}),

where
C_{2}^{j}
denotes the submatrix of

(C_{2B_{1}}|C_{2B_{2}})

corresponding to set of columns
j_{1},j_{2},\cdots,j_{n}

and all rows,
F^{i}
denotes the submatrix of

F

corresponding to set of columns
i_{1},i_{2},\cdots,i_{m}

and all rows.

We know that the determinants of
C_{2}^{j}
and
F^{i}
are nonzero
since their columns are linearly independent.
Therefore this term cannot be zero.
The determinant of
F^{i}
has only one nonzero term
which will have the form
\pm D_{k1}\times\cdots\times D_{kt}.

Suppose another pair
C_{2}^{j^{\prime}}
and
F^{i^{\prime}}
in the Laplace
expansion also has nonzero determinants. The determinant of
F^{i^{\prime}}
has only one nonzero term
which will have the form

\pm D_{k^{\prime}1}\times\cdots\times D_{k^{\prime}t^{\prime}}.

Since the columns of
F^{i}
as well as
F^{i^{\prime}}
form column bases of
the matrix
F,
it is clear that the sets
\{D_{k1},\cdots,D_{kt}\},\{D_{k^{\prime}1},\cdots,D_{k^{\prime}t^{\prime}}\},
are distinct. Therefore the Laplace expansion
will be a polynomial involving distinct subsets of
\{D_{1},\cdots D_{|B_{2}|},\}
in different terms and by algebraic independence
of these variables over
{\mathbb{Q}},
the polynomial cannot
take the value zero.
Thus the matrix in Equation
12

is nonsingular. We conclude that

r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{B_{1}B_{2}}+\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B_{1}B_{2}})=|B_{1}\uplus B_{2}|

and therefore that
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{B_{1}B_{2}},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B_{1}B_{2}}
have full sum property.

2. For handling the zero intersection case, when
r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{B_{1}B_{2}})+r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B_{1}B_{2}})\leq|B_{1}\uplus B_{2}|,

we can make use of the fact that if

r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{B_{1}B_{2}})+r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B_{1}B_{2}})\leq|B_{1}\uplus B_{2}|,

then
r((\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{B_{1}B_{2}})^{\perp})+r((\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B_{1}B_{2}})^{\perp})\geq|B_{1}\uplus B_{2}|.

Noting that

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}-D&I\end{pmatrix}

(13)

is a representative matrix for
(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B_{1}B_{2}})^{\perp},

we can use the above proof to show that
(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{B_{1}B_{2}})^{\perp},(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B_{1}B_{2}})^{\perp}

have full sum property.
Using part 4 of Theorem
17
,
we can then infer that

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{B_{1}B_{2}},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B_{1}B_{2}}

have zero intersection property.

3(a). The space
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}+\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}
has a representative matrix of the form

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}C_{A}&C_{B_{1}}&C_{B_{2}}\\
0&I&D\end{pmatrix},

(14)

where
(I|D)
is a representative matrix for
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}

and
(C_{A}|C_{B_{1}}|C_{B_{2}})
is a matrix over
{\mathbb{Q}}.

By using Laplace expansion as in part 1 above, we can see firstly that
every column base of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}+\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}
is independent in

\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB})\vee\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}).

Secondly, by the argument of part 1
that each term in the expansion
which involves diagonal entries of
D
has a distinct set of them,
using algebraic independence over
{\mathbb{Q}}
of diagonal entries of
D,

we can see that no cancellation of such terms can occur. Therefore every base of

\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB})\vee\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B})

is column independent in

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}+\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}.
We therefore have

\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}+\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B})=\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB})\vee\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}).

Since
(-D|I)
is a representative matrix of
(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B})^{\perp}

we can similarly prove that

\mbox{$\cal M$}((\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB})^{\perp}+(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B})^{\perp})=\mbox{$\cal M$}((\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB})^{\perp})\vee\mbox{$\cal M$}((\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B})^{\perp}).

3(bi)
\rightarrow
3(bii).
If
b^{1},b^{2}
are disjoint bases of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B},

such that
b^{1}\cup b^{2}\supseteq B,

it is clear that

r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}\circ B)+r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B})\geq|B|.

Further,
if
b^{1},b^{2}
are disjoint bases of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B},
such that

b^{1}\cup b^{2}\supseteq B,

A\cup B-b^{1},B-b^{2}
are disjoint bases of
(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB})^{\perp},(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B})^{\perp},
respectively and
(A\cup B-b^{1})\cup(B-b^{2})\supseteq B.

It is therefore clear that 3(bi) implies 3(bii).

3(bii)
\rightarrow
3(biii). Since
r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}\circ B)+r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B})\geq|B|,
by part 1 above, full sum property of
\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}\}
follows.
Since
r((\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB})^{\perp}\circ B)+r((\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B})^{\perp})\geq|B|,

we must have
|B|-r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}\times B)+|B|-r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B})\geq|B|,

i.e.,
r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}\times B)+r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B})\leq|B|

(using Theorems
1
,
2
).

Therefore, by part 2 above zero intersection property of
\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}\}
follows.

3(biii)
\rightarrow
3(biv).
This is part 1 of Theorem
56
.

3(biv)
\rightarrow
3(bi).
This is part 5 of Theorem
53
.
∎

The usual situation encountered in electrical circuit theory is one where
the device characteristic
is composed of independent voltage and current sources, resistors
and controlled sources, as in Equations
4
,
5
,
6
. In this case the vector space translate of the device
characteristic has additional variables which have no constraints on them
(eg. currents of independent and controlled voltage sources) and others which are zero
(eg. voltages of independent voltage sources and of controlling current branches).
Lemma
71
needs some further processing before it can be applied to determine the rigidity
of
\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}\},

where
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}:\equiv\mbox{$\cal F$}_{Z}\oplus{\mathbf{0}}_{Y}\oplus\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B},
and
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\circ A(X-Z)\times AB.

The most that can be said about this problem is given in Theorem
72
.
This theorem essentially reduces the problem, once certain necessary topological conditions
are satisfied, to checking the rigidity of the matroids associated with

\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}\}.

This checking can be done by applying the matroid union algorithm
[
3
]
.

Theorem 72
.

Let

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}
be vector spaces
on
A\uplus X,X,
respectively. Let
X=Z\uplus Y\uplus B.

Let
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}:\equiv\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\circ A(X-Z)\times AB.

Let
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}=\mbox{$\cal F$}_{Z}\oplus{\mathbf{0}}_{Y}\oplus\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}

and let
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\times Z={\mathbf{0}}_{Z},\ \mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\circ Y=\mbox{$\cal F$}_{Y}.

We then have
the following.

1.

(a)

\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}\}
is rigid iff
\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}\}

is rigid.

(b)

\{\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}),\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X})\}
is rigid iff

\{\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}),\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B})\}
is rigid.

(c)

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}.

(d)

\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX})\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X})=\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB})\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}).

2.

Let
\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}+\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B})=\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB})\vee\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B})
and let

\mbox{$\cal M$}((\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB})^{\perp}+(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B})^{\perp})=\mbox{$\cal M$}((\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB})^{\perp})\vee\mbox{$\cal M$}((\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B})^{\perp}).

We then have the following.

(a)

\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X})=\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX})\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X})=\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB})\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B})=\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}).

(b)

\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}\}
is rigid iff
\{\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}),\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X})\}
is rigid.

(c)

\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}\}\ (\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}\})
is rigid, iff there exists
a pair of disjoint bases
b^{1},b^{2}
of

\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}),\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B})\

(\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}),\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X})),
respectively, such that
(b^{1}\cup b^{2})\supseteq B\ ((b^{1}\cup b^{2})\supseteq X).

For such a pair
b^{1},b^{2},

b^{1}\cap A
is a column base of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}.

Proof.

1(a). We apply Theorem
21
,
taking
T=B,V=X-B=Y\uplus Z,W=A,\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{T}=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{B},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{V}=\mbox{$\cal F$}_{Z}\oplus{\mathbf{0}}_{Y}.

We then have
\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}\}
is rigid iff

\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX},\mbox{$\cal F$}_{Z}\oplus{\mathbf{0}}_{Y}\}
and
\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\leftrightarrow(\mbox{$\cal F$}_{Z}\oplus{\mathbf{0}}_{Y}),\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{B}\}
are rigid.
Let us examine the implication of
\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX},\mbox{$\cal F$}_{Z}\oplus{\mathbf{0}}_{Y}\}
being rigid.
We need
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\circ ZY+(\mbox{$\cal F$}_{Z}\oplus{\mathbf{0}}_{Y})=\mbox{$\cal F$}_{ZY}
(full sum property).
This is equivalent to
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\circ Y=\mbox{$\cal F$}_{Y}.

Next we need
(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\times ZY)\cap(\mbox{$\cal F$}_{Z}\oplus{\mathbf{0}}_{Y})={\mathbf{0}}_{ZY}
(zero intersection property).
This is equivalent to
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\times Z={\mathbf{0}}_{Z}.

Next
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\leftrightarrow(\mbox{$\cal F$}_{Z}\oplus{\mathbf{0}}_{Y})=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\circ A(X-Z)\times AB=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}.

The result follows.

1(b).
Since
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}=\mbox{$\cal F$}_{Z}\oplus{\mathbf{0}}_{Y}\oplus\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B},

we have
\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X})=\mbox{$\cal F$}_{Z}\oplus{\mathbf{0}}_{Y}\oplus\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}).

Since
\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\times Z)

=\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX})\times Z,

and
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\times Z={\mathbf{0}}_{Z},
we have

\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX})\times Z={\mathbf{0}}_{Z}.

Since
\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\circ Y)=\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX})\circ Y,

and
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\circ Y=\mbox{$\cal F$}_{Y},
we have

\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX})\circ Y=\mbox{$\cal F$}_{Y}.

Finally
\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB})=\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\circ A(X-Z)\times AB)

=\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX})\circ A(X-Z)\times AB.

The next part of the proof is a line by line translation of the proof of 1(a) above, taking

\mbox{$\cal M$}^{1}_{AX}:\equiv\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}),\mbox{$\cal M$}^{2}_{X}:\equiv\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}),\mbox{$\cal M$}^{1}_{AB}:\equiv\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}),\mbox{$\cal M$}^{2}_{B}:\equiv\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}).

We apply Theorem
55
,
taking
T=B,V=X-B=Y\uplus Z,W=A,\mbox{$\cal M$}_{T}=\mbox{$\cal M$}_{B},\mbox{$\cal M$}_{V}=\mbox{$\cal F$}_{Z}\oplus{\mathbf{0}}_{Y}.

We then have
\{\mbox{$\cal M$}^{1}_{AX},\mbox{$\cal M$}^{2}_{X}\}
is rigid iff

\{\mbox{$\cal M$}^{1}_{AX},\mbox{$\cal F$}_{Z}\oplus{\mathbf{0}}_{Y}\}
and
\{\mbox{$\cal M$}^{1}_{AX}\leftrightarrow(\mbox{$\cal F$}_{Z}\oplus{\mathbf{0}}_{Y}),\mbox{$\cal M$}_{B}\}
are rigid.
Let us examine the implication of
\{\mbox{$\cal M$}^{1}_{AX},\mbox{$\cal F$}_{Z}\oplus{\mathbf{0}}_{Y}\}
being rigid.
We need
\mbox{$\cal M$}^{1}_{AX}\circ ZY\vee(\mbox{$\cal F$}_{Z}+{\mathbf{0}}_{Y})=\mbox{$\cal F$}_{ZY}
(full sum property).
This is equivalent to
\mbox{$\cal M$}^{1}_{AX}\circ Y=\mbox{$\cal F$}_{Y}.

Next we need
(\mbox{$\cal M$}^{1}_{AX}\times ZY)\wedge(\mbox{$\cal F$}_{Z}+{\mathbf{0}}_{Y})={\mathbf{0}}_{ZY}
(zero intersection property).
This is equivalent to
\mbox{$\cal M$}^{1}_{AX}\times Z={\mathbf{0}}_{Z}.

Next
\mbox{$\cal M$}^{1}_{AX}\leftrightarrow(\mbox{$\cal F$}_{Z}\oplus{\mathbf{0}}_{Y})=\mbox{$\cal M$}^{1}_{AX}\circ A(X-Z)\times AB=\mbox{$\cal M$}^{1}_{AB}.

The result follows.

1(c). We observe that, by the definition of the matched composition operation,
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{WTV}\leftrightarrow(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{V}\oplus\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{T})

=(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{WTV}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{V})\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{T},
where
W,T,V
are pairwise disjoint.
It follows, using the substitutions in the proof of 1(a) above that

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}.

1(d). As in proof of 1(b), we take
\mbox{$\cal M$}^{1}_{AX}:\equiv\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}),\mbox{$\cal M$}^{2}_{X}:\equiv\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}),\mbox{$\cal M$}^{1}_{AB}:\equiv\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}),

\mbox{$\cal M$}^{2}_{B}:\equiv\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}).

We observe that, by the definition of the matched composition operation,

\mbox{$\cal M$}_{WTV}\leftrightarrow(\mbox{$\cal M$}_{V}\oplus\mbox{$\cal M$}_{T})=(\mbox{$\cal M$}_{WTV}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\cal M$}_{V})\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\cal M$}_{T},
where
W,T,V
are pairwise disjoint.
It follows, using the substitutions in the proof of 1(b) above that

\mbox{$\cal M$}^{1}_{AX}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\cal M$}^{2}_{X}=\mbox{$\cal M$}^{1}_{AB}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\cal M$}^{2}_{B}.

2(a). By part 2(b) of Theorem
56
, we have that

\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B})=\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB})\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}).

By 1(c) above,
we have
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}

and therefore
\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B})=\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}).

By 1(d) above
\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX})\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X})=\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB})\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}).

The result follows.

2(b).
By part 2(c) of Theorem
56
, under the conditions of
this part, we must have that
\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}\}
is rigid iff
\{\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}),\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B})\}
is rigid.
By part 1(a), 1(b) above, we have that
\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}\}
is rigid iff
\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}\}
is rigid
and
\{\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}),\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B})\}
is rigid iff
\{\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}),\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X})\}
is rigid.
The result follows.

2(c).
We have
\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}\}
is rigid iff
\{\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}),\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B})\}
is rigid.
Therefore, by
part 5 of Theorem
53
,

\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}\}
is rigid iff
there exists a pair of disjoint bases
b^{1},b^{2}
of

\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}),\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}),
respectively, such that
(b^{1}\cup b^{2})\supseteq B.

Now
(b^{1}\cup b^{2})
is a base of
\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB})\vee\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}),
that contains
a maximal intersection with
B
and therefore
b:\equiv(b^{1}\cup b^{2})\cap A=b^{1}\cap A

is a base of
\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB})\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}):\equiv(\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB})\vee\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}))\times A.

By part 2(b) of Theorem
56
, we have

\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B})=\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB})\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}).

Therefore,
b
is a column base of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}

(by part 1(c) above).

We have
\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}\}
is rigid iff
\{\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}),\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X})\}
is rigid
and
\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X})=\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX})\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}).

Arguing as above,
\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}\}
is rigid iff
there exists a pair of disjoint bases
b^{1},b^{2}
of

\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}),\mbox{$\cal M$}(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}),
respectively, such that
(b^{1}\cup b^{2})\supseteq X

and for such a pair
b^{1},b^{2},
we have
b^{1}\cap A
as a column base of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}.

∎

We use the following simple lemma in the proof of Theorem
68
.

Lemma 73
.

Let
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{X}
be a vector space on
X
and let
Y\subseteq X.

Let
T
be a column base of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{X}.

1.

Let
T\subseteq Y.

Then
T
is a column base of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{X}\circ Y.

2.

Let
T\supseteq(X-Y).

Then
T-(X-Y)
is a column base of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{X}\times Y.

Proof.

1. The columns
T
continue to remain independent in
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{X}\circ Y.

Since any column base
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{X}\circ Y
is contained in a column base of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{X},

the result follows.

2. We have
X-T
as a column cobase of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{X}
and therefore as a column base
of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{X}^{\perp}.
Now since
T\supseteq(X-Y),
we must have
X-T\subseteq Y.

By part 1 above
X-T
is a column cobase of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{X}^{\perp}\circ Y=(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{X}\times Y)^{\perp}.
Therefore
Y-(X-T)=(T-(X-Y))
is a column base of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{X}\times Y.

∎

Proof.

(
Proof of Theorem
68
)

For better readability, we introduce simplifying notation.
Let
A:\equiv P^{\prime}\uplus P",X:\equiv S^{\prime}\uplus S",

where

S=Y_{1}\uplus Z_{1}\uplus Y_{2}\uplus Z_{2}\uplus R\uplus E\uplus J,

B=B_{1}\uplus B_{2},
where
B_{1}:\equiv Y_{1}"\uplus Y_{2}^{\prime}\uplus R"

and

B_{2}:\equiv Z_{1}^{\prime}\uplus Z_{2}"\uplus R^{\prime}.

Let
X=X_{1}\uplus X_{2},
where
X_{1}:\equiv E"\uplus J^{\prime}\uplus Z_{1}"\uplus Z_{2}^{\prime}\uplus B_{1},

and let
X_{2}:\equiv E^{\prime}\uplus J"\uplus Y_{1}^{\prime}\uplus Y_{2}"\uplus B_{2}.

Note that, in the device characteristic
{\cal A}_{S^{\prime}S"},

X_{1}-B_{1}
are the variables without constraint,

X_{2}-B_{2}
are the ‘predetermined’ variables,

B_{1}
are the controlling variables and
B_{2}
are the controlled variables.
Observe that
|X_{1}|=|X_{2}|=|S|
and
|B_{1}|=|B_{2}|=|S|-|X_{1}-B_{1}|=|S|-|X_{2}-B_{2}|.

We define
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}:\equiv\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}S"}
and
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}:\equiv\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{P^{\prime}P"S^{\prime}S"}=(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP}))_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}}\oplus(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{i}({\cal G}_{SP}))_{S"P"}.

The solution space of combined Equations
4
,
5
,
6
is the affine space
{\cal A}^{2}_{X}:\equiv{\cal A}_{S^{\prime}S"}.
It can be seen that the vector space translate
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}

of
{\cal A}^{2}_{X}

has the form
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}S"}=\mbox{$\cal F$}_{E"Z_{1}"Z_{2}^{\prime}J^{\prime}}\oplus{\mathbf{0}}_{E^{\prime}Y_{1}^{\prime}Y_{2}"J"}\oplus\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B},

where
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}
has the representative matrix
(I|D)
with the partition corresponding to column division

B_{1},B_{2}
and with the diagonal entries of the diagonal matrix
D
being algebraically independent
over
{\mathbb{Q}}.

Note that
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}
is the source free device
characteristic, i.e., the voltage sources and current sources have zero values.
From the form of the device characteristic in Equations
4
,
5
,
6
, it is clear
that
r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X})=r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}S"})=|S|.

Therefore, by definition of proper multiports,
{\cal N}_{P}
is proper iff it is rigid.
By the definition of rigidity of multiports,
{\cal N}_{P}:\equiv(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX},{\cal A}^{2}_{X})
is rigid iff

\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}\}=\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{P^{\prime}P"S^{\prime}S"},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}S"}\}
is rigid.
Let
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}:\equiv\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\circ AB_{1}X_{2}\times AB.

The solution space of the topological constraints of the multiport
{\cal N}_{P}

has the form

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{P^{\prime}P"S^{\prime}S"}=(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP}))_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}}\oplus(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{i}({\cal G}_{SP}))_{S"P"}.

We now examine the nature of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}
and construct a column base for it.

A set of columns of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}

is a column base iff it has the form
t_{1}^{\prime}\uplus((S\uplus P)-t_{2})",
where

t_{1},t_{2}
are trees of
{\cal G}_{SP}
(Lemma
4
). Pick a tree
t
that contains
Y_{1}\uplus Z_{1}\uplus E
and does not intersect
Y_{2}\uplus Z_{2}\uplus J.
This is possible by Theorem
2
.
Choose as a column base of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}:\equiv(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP}))_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}}\oplus(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{i}({\cal G}_{SP}))_{S"P"},
the set

t^{\prime}\uplus((S\uplus P)-t)".

Let
t:\equiv Y_{1}\uplus Z_{1}\uplus E\uplus R_{1}\uplus P_{1},
where
P_{1}\subseteq P.

We have
S=Y_{1}\uplus Z_{1}\uplus E\uplus R\uplus Y_{2}\uplus Z_{2}\uplus J.

Therefore
\bar{t}:\equiv S\uplus P-t:\equiv Y_{2}\uplus Z_{2}\uplus J\uplus R_{2}\uplus P_{2},
where
P_{2}:\equiv P-P_{1},

R_{2}:\equiv R-R_{1}.

Before we begin the main part of the proof, we prove the following claim.

Claim.

The pair
\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}\}
is rigid.

Proof of Claim.

We will show that
(t-(E\uplus Y_{1}))^{\prime}\uplus(\bar{t}-(J\uplus Y_{2}))"
is a column base of

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}:\equiv\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\circ AB_{1}X_{2}\times AB_{1}B_{2}.

Since
X_{1}:\equiv E"\uplus J^{\prime}\uplus Z_{1}"\uplus Z_{2}^{\prime}\uplus B_{1},

X_{2}:\equiv E^{\prime}\uplus J"\uplus Y_{1}^{\prime}\uplus Y_{2}"\uplus B_{2},

t^{\prime}=Y^{\prime}_{1}\uplus Z^{\prime}_{1}\uplus E^{\prime}\uplus R^{\prime}_{1}\uplus P^{\prime}_{1},

\bar{t}"=Y"_{2}\uplus Z"_{2}\uplus J"\uplus R"_{2}\uplus P"_{2},

we see that

t^{\prime}\uplus\bar{t}"
does not intersect
X_{1}-B_{1}=E"\uplus J^{\prime}\uplus Z_{1}"\uplus Z_{2}^{\prime}.

Therefore,
t^{\prime}\uplus\bar{t}"
continues to remain a column base of

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\circ AB_{1}X_{2}
(by part 1 of Lemma
73
).

Next
X_{2}-B_{2}=E^{\prime}\uplus J"\uplus Y_{1}^{\prime}\uplus Y_{2}"\subseteq t^{\prime}\uplus\bar{t}",
Therefore
(t-(E\uplus Y_{1}))^{\prime}\uplus(\bar{t}-(J\uplus Y_{2}))"
is a column base of

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\circ AB_{1}X_{2}\times(AB_{1}X_{2}-(X_{2}-B_{2}))=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\circ AB_{1}X_{2}\times AB_{1}B_{2}

(by part 2 of Lemma
73
).

We now examine the column bases of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}.

Let
R=R_{1}\uplus R_{2},
where
R_{1}=t\cap R.

It can be verified that
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}
after column permutation within
B_{1}

and
B_{2}
has a representative matrix of the form
(I|D)\equiv\begin{pmatrix}I&0&\vline&D_{1}&0\\
0&I&\vline&0&\hat{R}\end{pmatrix},

where the identity matrix corresponds to columns
B_{1}:\equiv Y_{1}"\uplus Y_{2}^{\prime}\uplus R",

the diagonal matrix
D
corresponds to columns
B_{2}:\equiv Z_{1}^{\prime}\uplus Z_{2}"\uplus R^{\prime},

the top left identity submatrix corresponds to
Y_{1}"\uplus Y_{2}^{\prime},

the bottom right identity submatrix corresponds to
R".

We are given that the diagonal entries of the diagonal matrix
D
are algebraically
independent over
{\mathbb{Q}}.
In particular, they are nonzero.
Now since
B_{1}
is a column base for
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}

so would
(B_{1}-R_{2}")\uplus R_{2}^{\prime}=(Y_{1}"\uplus Y_{2}^{\prime}\uplus R_{2}^{\prime})\uplus R_{1}"
be.
Since
t:\equiv Y_{1}\uplus Z_{1}\uplus E\uplus R_{1}\uplus P_{1},\bar{t}:\equiv S\uplus P-t:\equiv Y_{2}\uplus Z_{2}\uplus J\uplus R_{2}\uplus P_{2},
it is clear that
(t-(Y_{1}\uplus E))^{\prime}\uplus(\bar{t}-(Y_{2}\uplus J))"
does not intersect this column base of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}.

Observe that
(t-(Y_{1}\uplus E))^{\prime}\uplus(\bar{t}-(Y_{2}\uplus J))"\uplus Y_{1}"\uplus Y_{2}^{\prime}\uplus R_{2}^{\prime}\uplus R_{1}"\supseteq Z_{1}^{\prime}\uplus R_{1}^{\prime}\uplus R_{2}^{\prime}\uplus Z_{2}"\uplus R_{1}"\uplus R_{2}"\uplus Y_{1}"\uplus Y_{2}^{\prime}=B.

Therefore we have disjoint column bases

(t-(Y_{1}\uplus E))^{\prime}\uplus(\bar{t}-(Y_{2}\uplus J))"

and

Y_{1}"\uplus Y_{2}^{\prime}\uplus R_{2}^{\prime}\uplus R_{1}"
of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}

respectively, which together cover
B.

By part 3(b) of Lemma
71
, it follows that
\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}\}
is rigid.

Proof of Claim ends

(
Proof of Theorem
68
continued.
)

1.
Since
{\cal N}_{P}
is given to be rigid,
\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}\}
must be rigid.
Therefore, we must have the full sum property satisfied,
i.e., that
r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}+\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X})\circ X=r((\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{P^{\prime}P"S^{\prime}S"}+\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}S"})\circ S^{\prime}S")=2|S|.

Now
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}S"}\circ E^{\prime}Y_{1}^{\prime}Y_{2}"J"={\mathbf{0}}_{E^{\prime}Y_{1}^{\prime}Y_{2}"J"}.
It follows that
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\circ E^{\prime}Y_{1}^{\prime}Y_{2}"J"

=(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP}))_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}}\circ E^{\prime}Y_{1}^{\prime}\oplus(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{i}({\cal G}_{SP}))_{S"P"}\circ Y_{2}"J"=\mbox{$\cal F$}_{E^{\prime}Y_{1}^{\prime}}\oplus\mbox{$\cal F$}_{Y_{2}"J"},

i.e. (using Lemma
4
), that

E\uplus Y_{1}
can be included in a tree of
{\cal G}_{SP}
and therefore is loop free and
J\uplus Y_{2}

can be included in a cotree of
{\cal G}_{SP}
and therefore is cutset free in
{\cal G}_{SP}.

2. We will first show that
\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}\}
is rigid iff
\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}\}
is rigid.

We are given that
Y_{1}\uplus Z_{1}\uplus E
is loop free and
Y_{2}\uplus Z_{2}\uplus J

is cutset free. Therefore there is a tree containing
Y_{1}\uplus Z_{1}\uplus E
and a cotree containing
Y_{2}\uplus Z_{2}\uplus J.
We therefore have,

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\times(X_{1}-B_{1})=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{P^{\prime}P"S^{\prime}S"}\times E"Z_{1}"Z_{2}^{\prime}J^{\prime}=(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP}))_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}}\times Z_{2}^{\prime}J^{\prime}\oplus(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{i}({\cal G}_{SP}))_{S"P"}\times Z_{1}"E"=

(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP}\times Z_{2}J))_{Z_{2}^{\prime}J^{\prime}}\oplus(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{i}({\cal G}_{SP}\circ Z_{1}E))_{Z_{1}"E"}={\mathbf{0}}_{Z_{2}^{\prime}J^{\prime}}\oplus{\mathbf{0}}_{E"Z_{1}"}={\mathbf{0}}_{E"Z_{1}"Z_{2}^{\prime}J^{\prime}}={\mathbf{0}}_{X_{1}-B_{1}}

and
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\circ(X_{2}-B_{2})

=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{P^{\prime}P"S^{\prime}S"}\circ E^{\prime}Y_{1}^{\prime}Y_{2}"J"=(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP}\circ EY_{1}))_{E^{\prime}Y_{1}^{\prime}}\oplus(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{i}({\cal G}_{SP}\times Y_{2}J))_{Y_{2}"J"}=\mbox{$\cal F$}_{E^{\prime}Y_{1}^{\prime}Y_{2}"J"}=\mbox{$\cal F$}_{X_{2}-B_{2}}
(using Lemma
4
).

Now we have,

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}:\equiv\mbox{$\cal F$}_{X_{1}-B_{1}}\oplus{\mathbf{0}}_{X_{2}-B_{2}}\oplus\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B},

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\times(X_{1}-B_{1})={\mathbf{0}}_{X_{1}-B_{1}},

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\circ(X_{2}-B_{2})=\mbox{$\cal F$}_{X_{2}-B_{2}}

and
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}:\equiv\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\circ AB_{1}X_{2}\times AB.

We can therefore apply Theorem
72
(taking
Z:\equiv X_{1}-B_{1},Y:\equiv X_{2}-B_{2}
)
and conclude that
\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}\}
is rigid iff
\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}\}
is rigid.

We have proved above the claim that
\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}\}
is rigid.
Therefore,
\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}\}
is rigid.

3. We have seen that when the conditions of part 2 are satisfied, there exists a tree
t
that
contains
E\uplus Y_{1}\uplus Z_{1}
and that does not intersect
J\uplus Y_{2}\uplus Z_{2}.

We have
t:\equiv Y_{1}\uplus Z_{1}\uplus E\uplus R_{1}\uplus P_{1},
where
P_{1}\subseteq P

and we have
S=Y_{1}\uplus Z_{1}\uplus E\uplus R\uplus Y_{2}\uplus Z_{2}\uplus J,

\bar{t}:\equiv S\uplus P-t=Y_{2}\uplus Z_{2}\uplus J\uplus R_{2}\uplus P_{2},
where
P_{2}:\equiv P-P_{1},

R_{2}:\equiv R-R_{1}.

We have seen that
b_{1}:\equiv t^{\prime}\uplus\bar{t}"
is a column base for

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}:\equiv\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{P^{\prime}P"S^{\prime}S"}=(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP}))_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}}\oplus(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{i}({\cal G}_{SP}))_{S"P"}.

We note that

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}S"}=\mbox{$\cal F$}_{E"Z_{1}"Z_{2}^{\prime}J^{\prime}}\oplus{\mathbf{0}}_{E^{\prime}Y_{1}^{\prime}Y_{2}"J"}\oplus\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B},

where
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}
has
column base
Y_{1}"\uplus Y_{2}^{\prime}\uplus R_{2}^{\prime}\uplus R_{1}".

Therefore
b_{2}:\equiv E"\uplus Z_{1}"\uplus Z_{2}^{\prime}\uplus J^{\prime}\uplus Y_{1}"\uplus Y_{2}^{\prime}\uplus R_{2}^{\prime}\uplus R_{1}"
is a column base for
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}.

It can be seen that
b_{1},b_{2}
are disjoint and together cover
X:\equiv S^{\prime}\uplus S".

By part 2 of Theorem
72
,

b^{1}\cap A:\equiv(t^{\prime}\uplus\bar{t}")\cap A=(t\cap P)^{\prime}\uplus(P-t)",

is a column base of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}.

Taking
v_{P_{1}^{\prime}}:\equiv(t\cap P)^{\prime},i_{P_{2}"}:\equiv(P-t)",

we see that
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\leftrightarrow{\cal A}^{2}_{X}
has a hybrid representation of the form

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}i_{P_{1}"}\\
v_{P_{2}^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}g_{11}&h_{12}\\
h_{21}&r_{22}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}v_{P_{1}^{\prime}}\\
i_{P_{2}"}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}s_{P_{1}"}\\
s_{P_{2}^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}.

(15)

Appendix C
Proof of Theorem
69

Lemma 74
.

Let
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"}
be Dirac.
We then have the following.

1.

r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"})=|S|+|P|=|S^{\prime}|+|P^{\prime}|=|S"|+|P"|.

2.

Let

T\subseteq S\uplus P
be such that

T^{\prime}
is a column base of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"}\circ S^{\prime}P^{\prime}.

Then

(S\uplus P-T)"
is a column base of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"}\times S"P"
and

T^{\prime}\uplus(S\uplus P-T)"
is a column base of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"}.

Proof.

1. We have
r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"})+r((\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"})^{\perp})=|S^{\prime}|+|P"|+|S"|+|P"|

(Theorem
1
). Since
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"}
is Dirac,
we must have
(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"})^{\perp}=(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"})_{S"P"S^{\prime}P^{\prime}}.

Since
S^{\prime},S"
are copies of
S
and
P^{\prime},P"
are copies of
P,

the result follows.

2. We denote
(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"})_{S"P"S^{\prime}P^{\prime}}
by
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{S"P"S^{\prime}P^{\prime}}.

Since
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"}
is Dirac, we have

(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"})^{\perp}=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{S"P"S^{\prime}P^{\prime}}.

We have that
(S\uplus P-T)^{\prime}
is a column cobase of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"}\circ S^{\prime}P^{\prime},

i.e., a column base of
(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"}\circ S^{\prime}P^{\prime})^{\perp}=(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"})^{\perp}\times S^{\prime}P^{\prime}=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{S"P"S^{\prime}P^{\prime}}\times S^{\prime}P^{\prime}.

Therefore,
(S\uplus P-T)"
is a column base of

(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{S"P"S^{\prime}P^{\prime}}\times S^{\prime}P^{\prime})_{S"P"}=(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{S"P"S^{\prime}P^{\prime}})_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"}\times S"P"=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"}\times S"P".
By Theorem
2
, part 4,

T^{\prime}\uplus(S\uplus P-T)"
is a column base of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"}.

∎

Proof.

(
Proof of Theorem
69
)

We will prove sufficiency assuming conditions in part (a).
The proof assuming part (b) is by interchanging prime and double prime for all
the variables in the proof assuming conditions in part (a).

For better readability, we introduce simplifying notation.
Let
A:\equiv P^{\prime}\uplus P",X:\equiv S^{\prime}\uplus S",

where

S=Y_{1}\uplus Z_{1}\uplus Y_{2}\uplus Z_{2}\uplus R\uplus E\uplus J,

B=B_{1}\uplus B_{2},
where
B_{1}:\equiv Y_{1}"\uplus Y_{2}^{\prime}\uplus R"

and

B_{2}:\equiv Z_{1}^{\prime}\uplus Z_{2}"\uplus R^{\prime}.

Let
X=X_{1}\uplus X_{2},
where
X_{1}:\equiv E"\uplus J^{\prime}\uplus Z_{1}"\uplus Z_{2}^{\prime}\uplus B_{1},

and let
X_{2}:\equiv E^{\prime}\uplus J"\uplus Y_{1}^{\prime}\uplus Y_{2}"\uplus B_{2}.

Note that, in the device characteristic
{\cal A}_{S^{\prime}S"},

X_{1}-B_{1}
are the variables without constraint,

X_{2}-B_{2}
are the ‘predetermined’ variables,

B_{1}
are the controlling variables and
B_{2}
are the controlled variables.
Observe that
|X_{1}|=|X_{2}|=|S|
and
|B_{1}|=|B_{2}|=|S|-|X_{1}-B_{1}|=|S|-|X_{2}-B_{2}|.

We define
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}:\equiv\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}S"}
and
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}:\equiv\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{P^{\prime}P"S^{\prime}S"}.

The solution space of combined Equations
4
,
5
,
6
is the affine space
{\cal A}^{2}_{X}:\equiv{\cal A}_{S^{\prime}S"}.
It can be seen that the vector space translate
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}

of
{\cal A}^{2}_{X}

has the form
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}S"}=\mbox{$\cal F$}_{E"Z_{1}"Z_{2}^{\prime}J^{\prime}}\oplus{\mathbf{0}}_{E^{\prime}Y_{1}^{\prime}Y_{2}"J"}\oplus\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B},

where
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}
has the representative matrix
(I|D)
with the partition corresponding to column division

B_{1},B_{2}
and with the diagonal entries of the diagonal matrix
D
being algebraically independent
over
{\mathbb{Q}}.

Note that
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}
is the source free device
characteristic, i.e., the voltage sources and current sources have zero values.
By the definition of rigidity of pairs of affine spaces,
{\cal N}^{g}_{P}:\equiv(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX},{\cal A}^{2}_{X})
is rigid iff

\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}\}=\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{P^{\prime}P"S^{\prime}S"},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}S"}\}
is rigid.
Let
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}:\equiv\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\circ AB_{1}X_{2}\times AB.

We first construct a column base for the Dirac space
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{P^{\prime}P"S^{\prime}S"}.

By Lemma
74
, we have that a set of columns of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}

is a column base if it has the form
T^{\prime}\uplus(S\uplus P-T)",

where
T^{\prime}
is a column base of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"}\circ S^{\prime}P^{\prime}.

Pick a column base
T^{\prime}
of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"}\circ S^{\prime}P^{\prime}
that contains
Y^{\prime}_{1}\uplus Z^{\prime}_{1}\uplus E^{\prime}
and does not intersect
Y^{\prime}_{2}\uplus Z^{\prime}_{2}\uplus J^{\prime}.

This is possible by part 7 of Theorem
2
.
Choose the set
T^{\prime}\uplus(S\uplus P-T)"
as a column base of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}:\equiv\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"}.

Before we begin the main part of the proof, we prove the following claim.

Claim.

The pair
\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}\}
is rigid.

Proof of Claim.

Let
T:\equiv Y_{1}\uplus Z_{1}\uplus E\uplus R_{1}\uplus P_{1},
where
P_{1}\subseteq P.

We have
S=Y_{1}\uplus Z_{1}\uplus E\uplus R\uplus Y_{2}\uplus Z_{2}\uplus J,

Therefore
\bar{T}:\equiv S\uplus P-T=Y_{2}\uplus Z_{2}\uplus J\uplus R_{2}\uplus P_{2},
where
P_{2}:\equiv P-P_{1},

R_{2}:\equiv R-R_{1}.

We will show that
(T-(E\uplus Y_{1}))^{\prime}\uplus(\bar{T}-(J\uplus Y_{2}))"
is a column base of

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}.

We have
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}:\equiv\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\circ AB_{1}X_{2}\times AB_{1}B_{2}.

Since
X_{1}:\equiv E"\uplus J^{\prime}\uplus Z_{1}"\uplus Z_{2}^{\prime}\uplus B_{1},

X_{2}:\equiv E^{\prime}\uplus J"\uplus Y_{1}^{\prime}\uplus Y_{2}"\uplus B_{2},

T^{\prime}=Y^{\prime}_{1}\uplus Z^{\prime}_{1}\uplus E^{\prime}\uplus R^{\prime}_{1}\uplus P^{\prime}_{1},

\bar{T}"=Y"_{2}\uplus Z"_{2}\uplus J"\uplus R"_{2}\uplus P"_{2},

we see that

T^{\prime}\uplus\bar{T}"
does not intersect
X_{1}-B_{1}=E"\uplus J^{\prime}\uplus Z_{1}"\uplus Z_{2}^{\prime}.

Therefore,
T^{\prime}\uplus\bar{T}"
continues to remain a column base of

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\circ AB_{1}X_{2}
(by part 1 of Lemma
73
).

Next
X_{2}-B_{2}=E^{\prime}\uplus J"\uplus Y_{1}^{\prime}\uplus Y_{2}"\subseteq T^{\prime}\uplus\bar{T}",
Therefore
(T-(E\uplus Y_{1}))^{\prime}\uplus(\bar{T}-(J\uplus Y_{2}))"
is a column base of

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB}=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\circ AB_{1}X_{2}\times(AB_{1}X_{2}-(X_{2}-B_{2}))=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\circ AB_{1}X_{2}\times AB_{1}B_{2}

(by part 2 of Lemma
73
).

We now examine the column bases of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}.

We have
R=R_{1}\uplus R_{2},
where
R_{1}=T\cap R.

It can be verified that
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}
after column permutation within
B_{1}

and
B_{2}
has a representative matrix of the form
(I|D)\equiv\begin{pmatrix}I&0&\vline&D_{1}&0\\
0&I&\vline&0&\hat{R}\end{pmatrix},

where the identity matrix corresponds to columns
B_{1}:\equiv Y_{1}"\uplus Y_{2}^{\prime}\uplus R",

the diagonal matrix
D
corresponds to columns
B_{2}:\equiv Z_{1}^{\prime}\uplus Z_{2}"\uplus R^{\prime},

the top left identity submatrix corresponds to
Y_{1}"\uplus Y_{2}^{\prime},

the bottom right identity submatrix corresponds to
R".

We are given that the diagonal entries of the diagonal matrix
D
are algebraically
independent over
{\mathbb{Q}}.
In particular, they are nonzero.
Now since
B_{1}
is a column base for
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}

so would
(B_{1}-R_{2}")\uplus R_{2}^{\prime}=(Y_{1}"\uplus Y_{2}^{\prime}\uplus R_{2}^{\prime})\uplus R_{1}"
be.
Since
T:\equiv Y_{1}\uplus Z_{1}\uplus E\uplus R_{1}\uplus P_{1},

\bar{T}:\equiv S\uplus P-T:\equiv Y_{2}\uplus Z_{2}\uplus J\uplus R_{2}\uplus P_{2},
it is clear that
(T-(Y_{1}\uplus E))^{\prime}\uplus(\bar{T}-(Y_{2}\uplus J))"
does not intersect this column base.

Observe that
(T-(Y_{1}\uplus E))^{\prime}\uplus(\bar{T}-(Y_{2}\uplus J))"\uplus Y_{1}"\uplus Y_{2}^{\prime}\uplus R_{2}^{\prime}\uplus R_{1}"\supseteq Z_{1}^{\prime}\uplus R_{1}^{\prime}\uplus R_{2}^{\prime}\uplus Z_{2}"\uplus R_{1}"\uplus R_{2}"\uplus Y_{1}"\uplus Y_{2}^{\prime}=B.

Therefore we have disjoint column bases

(T-(Y_{1}\uplus E))^{\prime}\uplus(\bar{T}-(Y_{2}\uplus J))"

and

Y_{1}"\uplus Y_{2}^{\prime}\uplus R_{2}^{\prime}\uplus R_{1}"
of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}

respectively, which together cover
B.

By part 3(b) of Lemma
71
, it follows that
\{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AB},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}\}
is rigid.

Proof of Claim ends

1.

3. We have seen that when the conditions of part 2(a) are satisfied, there exists a column base

T:\equiv Y_{1}\uplus Z_{1}\uplus E\uplus R_{1}\uplus P_{1},
where

and we have
S=Y_{1}\uplus Z_{1}\uplus E\uplus R\uplus Y_{2}\uplus Z_{2}\uplus J,

\bar{T}:\equiv S\uplus P-T=Y_{2}\uplus Z_{2}\uplus J\uplus R_{2}\uplus P_{2},

P_{2}:\equiv P-P_{1},

We have seen that
b_{1}:\equiv T^{\prime}\uplus\bar{T}"
is a column base for

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}:\equiv\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{P^{\prime}P"S^{\prime}S"}.

We note that

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}S"}=\mbox{$\cal F$}_{E"Z_{1}"Z_{2}^{\prime}J^{\prime}}\oplus{\mathbf{0}}_{E^{\prime}Y_{1}^{\prime}Y_{2}"J"}\oplus\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B},

where
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{B}
has

Y_{1}"\uplus Y_{2}^{\prime}\uplus R_{2}^{\prime}\uplus R_{1}".

b_{2}:\equiv E"\uplus Z_{1}"\uplus Z_{2}^{\prime}\uplus J^{\prime}\uplus Y_{1}"\uplus Y_{2}^{\prime}\uplus R_{2}^{\prime}\uplus R_{1}"
is a column base for
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}.

It can be seen that
b_{1},b_{2}
are disjoint and together cover
