By part 2(c) of Theorem
72
,

b_{1}\cap A:\equiv(T^{\prime}\uplus\bar{T}")\cap A=(T\cap P)^{\prime}\uplus(P-T)",

is a column base of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{2}_{X}.

Taking
{P_{1}^{\prime}}:\equiv(T\cap P)^{\prime}=b_{1}\cap P^{\prime},{P_{2}"}:\equiv(P-T)"=b_{1}\cap P",

we see that
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{1}_{AX}\leftrightarrow{\cal A}^{2}_{X}
has a hybrid representation of the form

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}i_{P_{1}"}\\
v_{P_{2}^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}g_{11}&h_{12}\\
h_{21}&r_{22}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}v_{P_{1}^{\prime}}\\
i_{P_{2}"}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}s_{P_{1}"}\\
s_{P_{2}^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}.

(16)

∎

Appendix D
Port transformation for general multiports

The Implicit Inversion Theorem (Theorem
8
) has a special feature which is suited to electrical
network applications, viz., the fact that in the equation

{\cal V}_{SP}\leftrightarrow{\cal K}_{S}={\cal K}_{P},
because
{\cal V}_{SP}
is a vector space, we can
relate
{\cal K}_{S},{\cal K}_{P},
even if they are very general. In electrical networks,
this permits us to speak port characteristics for multiports with very general
device characteristics because the topological constraints are linear.
We present below, a discussion on ‘port transformation’ for general
multiports with arbitrary device characteristic.

The multiport behaviour
{{\cal K}}_{P^{\prime}P"}
of
{\cal N}_{P}
on graph
{\cal G}_{SP},
with an arbitrary collection
of vectors
{\cal K}_{S^{\prime}S"}
as its device characteristic, is given by

{{\cal K}}_{P^{\prime}P"}=[(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({{\cal G}_{SP}}))_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}}\oplus(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{i}({{\cal G}_{SP}}))_{S"P"}]\leftrightarrow{{\cal K}}_{S^{\prime}S"}.

A natural question is
‘given
{{\cal K}}_{P^{\prime}P"},
what can we infer about

{{\cal K}}_{S^{\prime}S"}?
’

The answer to a more general form of this question is immediate from Theorem
8
.
We state this as a corollary to that theorem.
(Note that the roles of sets
S,P
in Theorem
8

and the corollary below, are interchanged.)

Corollary 75
.

1. Let
{\cal V}_{SP}
be a vector space on
S\uplus P
and
{\cal K}_{S}
be an arbitrary
collection of vectors on
S.

Let
{\cal V}_{SP}\leftrightarrow{\cal K}_{S}={\cal K}_{P}.
Then
{\cal V}_{SP}\leftrightarrow{\cal K}_{P}=({\cal K}_{S}+{\cal V}_{SP}\times S)\cap{\cal V}_{SP}\circ S=({\cal K}_{S}\cap{\cal V}_{SP}\circ S)+{\cal V}_{SP}\times S.

2. Let
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"}:\equiv(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({{\cal G}_{SP}}))_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}}\oplus(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{i}({{\cal G}_{SP}}))_{S"P"}
and let

{{\cal K}}_{P^{\prime}P"}=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"}\leftrightarrow{{\cal K}}_{S^{\prime}S"}.

Then

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"}\leftrightarrow{{\cal K}}_{P^{\prime}P"}=[{{\cal K}}_{S^{\prime}S"}\cap((\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({{\cal G}_{SP}}\circ S))_{S^{\prime}}\oplus(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{i}({{\cal G}_{SP}}\times S))_{S"})]+[(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({{\cal G}_{SP}}\times S))_{S^{\prime}}\oplus(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{i}({{\cal G}_{SP}}\circ S))_{S"}].

Proof.

1. Let
\hat{{\cal K}}_{S}:\equiv({\cal K}_{S}+{\cal V}_{SP}\times S)\cap{\cal V}_{SP}\circ S.

Since
{\cal V}_{SP}\circ S
is a vector space and
{\cal V}_{SP}\times S\subseteq{\cal V}_{SP}\circ S,

a vector
f_{S}+g_{S},g_{S}\in{\cal V}_{SP}\times S,
belongs to
{\cal V}_{SP}\circ S
iff

f_{S}\in{\cal V}_{SP}\circ S.
Therefore,
it is clear that

({\cal K}_{S}+{\cal V}_{SP}\times S)\cap{\cal V}_{SP}\circ S=({\cal K}_{S}\cap{\cal V}_{SP}\circ S)+{\cal V}_{SP}\times S.

We note that for any collection of vectors
{\cal K}^{\prime}_{S},

we must have

{\cal V}_{SP}\leftrightarrow{\cal K}^{\prime}_{S}={\cal V}_{SP}\leftrightarrow({\cal K}^{\prime}_{S}+{\cal V}_{SP}\times S)={\cal V}_{SP}\leftrightarrow({\cal K}^{\prime}_{S}\cap{\cal V}_{SP}\circ S).

Therefore,
{\cal V}_{SP}\leftrightarrow{\cal K}_{S}={\cal V}_{SP}\leftrightarrow\hat{{\cal K}}_{S}={\cal K}_{P}.

Since
\hat{{\cal K}}_{S}\subseteq{\cal V}_{SP}\circ S
and
\hat{{\cal K}}_{S}\supseteq\hat{{\cal K}}_{S}+{\cal V}_{SP}\times S,
we know by Theorem
8
, that

{\cal V}_{SP}\leftrightarrow({\cal V}_{SP}\leftrightarrow\hat{{\cal K}}_{S})=\hat{{\cal K}}_{S}.

Since
{\cal V}_{SP}\leftrightarrow\hat{{\cal K}}_{S}={\cal K}_{P},
this proves the result.

2. We have
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"}\circ S^{\prime}S"=(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({{\cal G}_{SP}})\circ S)_{S^{\prime}}\oplus(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{i}({{\cal G}_{SP}})\circ S)_{S"}=(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({{\cal G}_{SP}}\circ S))_{S^{\prime}}\oplus(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{i}({{\cal G}_{SP}}\times S))_{S"}

and

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"}\times S^{\prime}S"=(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({{\cal G}_{SP}})\times S)_{S^{\prime}}\oplus(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{i}({{\cal G}_{SP}})\times S)_{S"}=(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({{\cal G}_{SP}}\times S))_{S^{\prime}}\oplus(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{i}({{\cal G}_{SP}}\circ S))_{S"},

using Lemma
4
.
The result follows using the previous part, taking
P:\equiv P^{\prime}\uplus P".\ S:\equiv S^{\prime}\uplus S".

∎

It is immediate from Corollary
75
, that

{\cal V}_{SP}\leftrightarrow{\cal K}_{S}
and
{\cal V}_{SQ}\leftrightarrow{\cal K}_{S}
provide us with the same
information about
{\cal K}_{S},
if
{\cal V}_{SP}\times S={\cal V}_{SQ}\times S

and
{\cal V}_{SP}\circ S={\cal V}_{SQ}\circ S.

This is so because, if the latter conditions are satisfied, then we have
{\cal V}_{SP}\leftrightarrow({\cal V}_{SP}\leftrightarrow{\cal K}_{S})={\cal V}_{SQ}\leftrightarrow({\cal V}_{SQ}\leftrightarrow{\cal K}_{S})=({\cal K}_{S}\cap{\cal V}_{SP}\circ S)+{\cal V}_{SP}\times S.

This fact is useful to note for a generalized multiport
and leads to the following definition.

Definition 76
.

Let
{\cal N}^{g}_{P}:\equiv(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"},{\cal K}_{S^{\prime}S"}),
be a generalized multiport.
The collection of vectors

({\cal K}_{S^{\prime}S"}\cap\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"}\circ S^{\prime}S")+\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"}\times S^{\prime}S",
is the
internal model
of
{\cal N}^{g}_{P},

available from its ports.

It is natural to regard two generalized multiports which have the same
internal model available at their ports, as port transformations of each
other. Using Corollary
75
, this leads
to the following definition.

Definition 77
.

We say
{\cal V}_{SP},{\cal V}_{SQ}
are
port transformations
of each other with respect
to
S,

iff

{\cal V}_{SP}\times S={\cal V}_{SQ}\times S

and
{\cal V}_{SP}\circ S={\cal V}_{SQ}\circ S.

We say
{\cal G}_{SP},{\cal G}_{SQ}
are port transformations of each other with respect
to
S,

iff

{\cal G}_{SP}\times S={\cal G}_{SQ}\times S

and
{\cal G}_{SP}\circ S={\cal G}_{SQ}\circ S.

Let
{\cal N}^{g}_{P}:\equiv(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"},{\cal K}_{S^{\prime}S"}),
be a generalized multiport.
A generalized multiport

{\cal N}^{g}_{Q}:\equiv(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}Q^{\prime}S"Q"},{\cal K}_{S^{\prime}S"}),
is a port transformation of
{\cal N}^{g}_{P}
iff
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}Q^{\prime}S"Q"}\times Q^{\prime}Q"=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"}\times P^{\prime}P"
and
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}Q^{\prime}S"Q"}\circ Q^{\prime}Q"=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"}\circ P^{\prime}P".

A multiport
{\cal N}_{Q}:\equiv({\cal G}_{SP},{\cal K}_{S^{\prime}S"})
is a port transformation
of the multiport
{\cal N}_{P}:\equiv({\cal G}_{SQ},{\cal K}_{S^{\prime}S"})

iff
{\cal G}_{SP}\times S={\cal G}_{SQ}\times S

and
{\cal G}_{SP}\circ S={\cal G}_{SQ}\circ S.

The next lemma, which follows from Lemma
4
, emphasizes that the Definition
77

is consistently worded.

Lemma 78
.

Let
{\cal G}_{SP},{\cal G}_{SQ}
be graphs on
S\uplus P,

S\uplus Q,

respectively.

Let
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"}:\equiv(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({{\cal G}_{SP}})_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}}\oplus(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{i}({{\cal G}_{SP}})_{S"P"}
and let

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}Q^{\prime}S"Q"}:\equiv(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({{\cal G}_{SQ}})_{S^{\prime}Q^{\prime}}\oplus(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{i}({{\cal G}_{SQ}})_{S"Q"}.

Then
{\cal G}_{SQ}
is a port transformation of
{\cal G}_{SP}
with respect to
S,
iff

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}Q^{\prime}S"Q"}
is a port transformation of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}S"P"}
with respect to
S^{\prime}S".

The next lemma speaks of a useful way of relating port transformations through
a suitable matched composition.

Lemma 79
.

Let
S,P,Q,
be pairwise disjoint.
The spaces
{\cal V}_{SP},{\cal V}_{SQ}
are port transformations of each other with respect
to
S,
iff
there exists
{\cal V}_{PQ}
such that
{\cal V}_{SP}\leftrightarrow{\cal V}_{PQ}={\cal V}_{SQ}
and
such that

{\cal V}_{SP}\circ P={\cal V}_{PQ}\circ P,{\cal V}_{SP}\times P={\cal V}_{PQ}\times P.

Proof.

(if) It is easily seen
that
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{AB}\leftrightarrow(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{BC}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{C})=(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{AB}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{BC})\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{C},

when
A,B,C,
are pairwise disjoint.
It is also easy to see that
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{AB}\leftrightarrow(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{AB}\times B)=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{AB}\times A

and
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{AB}\leftrightarrow(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{AB}\circ B)=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{AB}\circ A.

Therefore,

{\cal V}_{SQ}\times S:\equiv{\cal V}_{SQ}\leftrightarrow{\mathbf{0}}_{Q}=({\cal V}_{SP}\leftrightarrow{\cal V}_{PQ})\leftrightarrow{\mathbf{0}}_{Q}={\cal V}_{SP}\leftrightarrow({\cal V}_{PQ}\leftrightarrow{\mathbf{0}}_{Q})={\cal V}_{SP}\leftrightarrow({\cal V}_{PQ}\times P)

={\cal V}_{SP}\leftrightarrow({\cal V}_{SP}\times P)={\cal V}_{SP}\times S.

By replacing
{\mathbf{0}}_{Q}
in the above argument by
\mbox{$\cal F$}_{Q},
we see that

{\cal V}_{SQ}\circ S={\cal V}_{SP}\circ S.

(only if) We define
{\cal V}_{PQ}:\equiv{\cal V}_{SP}\leftrightarrow{\cal V}_{SQ}.

Since
{\cal V}_{SP}\leftrightarrow{\cal V}_{SQ}={\cal V}_{PQ}
and
{\cal V}_{SP}\times S={\cal V}_{SQ}\times S,\ {\cal V}_{SP}\circ S={\cal V}_{SQ}\circ S,

by Theorem
9
, we have
{\cal V}_{SP}\leftrightarrow{\cal V}_{PQ}={\cal V}_{SQ}.

Next, starting from
{\cal V}_{SP}\leftrightarrow{\cal V}_{SQ}={\cal V}_{PQ},

by using the argument of the previous part, but interchanging the roles
of
S,P
and
{\cal V}_{PQ},{\cal V}_{SQ},

we get

{\cal V}_{SP}\circ P={\cal V}_{PQ}\circ P,{\cal V}_{SP}\times P={\cal V}_{PQ}\times P.

∎

Remark 11
.

We have taken
S,P,Q,
to be pairwise disjoint in Lemma
79
. But one of the convenient ways of changing the set of ports
is to define
{\cal V}_{SQ}:\equiv{\cal V}_{SP}\leftrightarrow({\mathbf{0}}_{P_{2}}\oplus\mbox{$\cal F$}_{P_{3}}),\ P:\equiv P_{1}\uplus P_{2}\uplus P_{3},

taking care
to keep
{\cal V}_{SP}\circ S={\cal V}_{PQ}\circ S,{\cal V}_{SP}\times S={\cal V}_{PQ}\times S.

In this case, to apply Lemma
79
,
we could define
\tilde{P}
to be a disjoint copy of
P,
disjoint also
from
S,
take

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S\tilde{P}}\equiv({\cal V}_{SP})_{S\tilde{P}}
and

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{SQ}:\equiv\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S\tilde{P}}\leftrightarrow({\mathbf{0}}_{\tilde{P}_{2}}\oplus\mbox{$\cal F$}_{\tilde{P}_{3}}),\ \tilde{P}:\equiv\tilde{P}_{1}\uplus\tilde{P}_{2}\uplus\tilde{P}_{3},\ Q:\equiv\tilde{P}_{1}.

A natural question next would be ‘for a given vector space
{\cal V}_{SP},

what is the port transformation
{\cal V}_{SQ}
with minimum size for the
set of ports
Q
and how to construct it efficiently?’

A lower bound is immediate.

Lemma 80
.

Let
{\cal V}_{SP}
be a vector space. Then
|P|\geq r({\cal V}_{SP}\circ S)-r({\cal V}_{SP}\times S).

Proof.

We have, by Theorem
2
,

r({\cal V}_{SP})=r({\cal V}_{SP}\circ S)+r({\cal V}_{SP}\times P)=r({\cal V}_{SP}\times S)+r({\cal V}_{SP}\circ P).

Therefore,
r({\cal V}_{SP}\circ S)-r({\cal V}_{SP}\times S)=r({\cal V}_{SP}\circ P)-r({\cal V}_{SP}\times P).
But
|P|\geq r({\cal V}_{SP}\circ P)
and
r({\cal V}_{SP}\times P)\geq 0.

The result follows.
∎

We now present a simple algorithm for building the port transformation
with minimum port size, where we actually achieve the lower bound.

Algorithm II

Input:
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{TR}

Output:
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{T\tilde{R}},

with
|\tilde{R}|=r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{T\tilde{R}}\circ T)-r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{T\tilde{R}}\times T),

such that

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{T\tilde{R}}\times T=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{TR}\times T,\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{T\tilde{R}}\circ T=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{TR}\circ T.

Step 1. We build a representative matrix
B
of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{TR},
in which

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{TR}\circ T,\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{TR}\times T,\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{TR}\circ R,\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{TR}\times R,
are visible
as shown below.

\displaystyle B=\begin{pmatrix}B_{1T}&{\mathbf{0}}_{1R}\\
B_{2T}&B_{2R}\\
{\mathbf{0}}_{3T}&B_{3R}\end{pmatrix}.

(17)

Here
B_{1T}
is a representative matrix of

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{TR}\times T,

(B_{1T}^{T}|B_{2T}^{T})^{T}
is a representative matrix of

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{TR}\circ T,

B_{3R}
is a representative matrix of

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{TR}\times R,

(B_{2R}^{T}|B_{3R}^{T})^{T}
is a representative matrix of

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{TR}\circ R.

We note that the rows of
B_{2T},B_{2R}
are linearly independent
and their number

equals
r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{T{R}}\circ T)-r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{T{R}}\times T)=r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{T{R}}\circ R)-r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{T{R}}\times R).

Step 2. Let
R=\tilde{R}\uplus R_{2},
where
\tilde{R}
is a maximal linearly independent
subset of columns of

(B_{2R})

and let
(B_{2R})=(B_{2\tilde{R}}|B_{2{R_{2}}}).

Take
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{T\tilde{R}}
to have the representative matrix
\tilde{B}
shown below.

\displaystyle\tilde{B}=\begin{pmatrix}B_{1T}&{\mathbf{0}}_{1\tilde{R}}\\
B_{2T}&B_{2\tilde{R}}\end{pmatrix}.

(18)

We have

|\tilde{R}|=r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{TR}\circ T)-r(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{TR}\times T),

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{T\tilde{R}}\times T=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{TR}\times T,\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{T\tilde{R}}\circ T=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{TR}\circ T.

We give below a simple graph based algorithm for minimising
the number of ports of a multiport. As can be seen, the algorithm
is linear time on the number of edges in the graph.
We use the term ‘tree of a graph’ to mean a maximal loop free set
in the graph, even if the graph is not connected.

Algorithm III

Input: A graph
{\cal G}_{SP}
on edge set
S\uplus P.

Output: A graph
{\cal G}_{S\tilde{P}}
on edge set
S\uplus\tilde{P},

such that

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{S\tilde{P}})\times S=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{S{P}})\times S,\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{S\tilde{P}})\circ S=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{S{P}})\circ S,

|\tilde{P}|=r({\cal G}_{SP}\circ S)-r({\cal G}_{SP}\times S).

Step 1. Build a tree
t_{1}
of
{\cal G}_{SP}\circ S,
and grow this into a tree

t_{1}\uplus t_{2}
of
{\cal G}_{SP}.

Step 2. Grow
t_{2}
into a tree
\hat{t}_{2}
of
{\cal G}_{SP}\circ P.

Step 3. Take
\tilde{P}:\equiv\hat{t}_{2}-{t}_{2}.
Build the graph
{\cal G}_{S\tilde{P}}:\equiv{\cal G}_{SP}\circ(S\uplus\hat{t}_{2})\times(S\uplus(\hat{t}_{2}-{t}_{2})).

STOP

Justification for Algorithm
III

We use the following facts from graph theory.

Fact 0.
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}),\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{i}({\cal G})
are determined entirely by
the set of circuits (cutsets) and the
relative orientation of the edges with respect to the circuits (cutsets) of
{\cal G}.

Fact 1. For any graph
{\cal G}_{SP},
if a tree
t
contains a tree

t_{1}
of
{\cal G}_{SP}\circ S,
then
t-t_{1}
is a tree of
{\cal G}_{SP}\times P.

Fact 2. If
t_{1}
is a tree of
{\cal G}_{SP}\circ S
and
t_{2},
a tree of

{\cal G}_{SP}\times P,
then
t_{1}\uplus t_{2}
is a tree of
{\cal G}_{SP}.

If
t_{2}
is contracted in
{\cal G}_{SP},
loops of
{\cal G}_{SP}
within
S,
continue
to remain as loops, the relative orientation of edges with respect to that of
the loops remains unchanged, and further, no new loops
are created in
S.

Equivalently,
[\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP}\times(S\uplus(P-t_{2})))]\circ S=(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP}))\circ S.

Fact 3. If
\hat{t}_{2}
is a tree of
{\cal G}_{SP}\circ P,
then if
P-\hat{t}_{2}
is deleted
in
{\cal G}_{SP},

cutsets of
{\cal G}_{SP}
within
S,
continue
to remain as cutsets, the relative orientation of edges with respect to that of
the cutsets remains unchanged, and further, no new cutsets
are created in
S.

Equivalently,
[\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP}\circ(S\uplus\hat{t}_{2}))]\times S=(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP}))\times S.

We have
{\cal G}_{S\tilde{P}}:\equiv{\cal G}_{SP}\circ(S\uplus\hat{t}_{2})\times(S\uplus(\hat{t}_{2}-t_{2}))={\cal G}_{SP}\times(S\uplus(P-t_{2}))\circ(S\uplus(\hat{t}_{2}-t_{2})).

Therefore,
[\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{S\tilde{P}})]\circ S=[\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP}\times(S\uplus(P-t_{2}))\circ(S\uplus(\hat{t}_{2}-t_{2})))]\circ S=[\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP}\times(S\uplus(P-t_{2})))]\circ S=[\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP})]\circ S

(by Fact 2. above).

Next,
[\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{S\tilde{P}})]\times S=[\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP}\circ(S\uplus\hat{t}_{2}))\times(S\uplus(\hat{t}_{2}-t_{2}))]\times S=[\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP}\circ(S\uplus\hat{t}_{2}))]\times S=[\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP})]\times S

(by Fact 3. above).

\ \ \ \ \ \Box

Remark 12
.

We note that in Algorithm
III
, if
{\cal G}_{SP}
is connected, then
{\cal G}_{S\tilde{P}}\circ S
would always be connected,
but
{\cal G}_{S{P}}\circ S
may not be.
However
[\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{S\tilde{P}})]\circ S
would always equal
[\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{S{P}})]\circ S.

D.1
Computing port behaviour from the behaviour of port minimized version

In Section
7.3
, we reduced the problem of testing rigidity
of a multiport with gyrators, resistors, controlled and independent sources,
to that of testing independence of certain columns
of a vector space which is the multiport behaviour of another multiport
with a Dirac structure as its device characterisitc.
In such cases, it would be convenient
to reduce the problem to that of the independence of a smaller set of columns
of the behaviour of a port minimized version.
We show how to do this in this section.

We use the notation of Algorithm
III
.

Let
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{SP}:\equiv\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP}),
and let
t_{1},t_{2},\hat{t}_{2},
be trees of
{\cal G}_{SP}\circ S,{\cal G}_{SP}\times P,{\cal G}_{SP}\circ P,
respectively with
t_{2}\subseteq\hat{t}_{2}.

Let the ‘minimized ports’
\hat{t}_{2}-t_{2}
be denoted by
\tilde{P}.

Let
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{P^{\prime}P"}:\equiv[({\cal V}_{SP})_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}}\oplus({\cal V}_{SP}^{\perp})_{S"P"}]\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}S"}
and let
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}\tilde{P}"}

:\equiv[(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S\tilde{P}})_{S^{\prime}\tilde{P}^{\prime}}\oplus(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S\tilde{P}}^{\perp})_{S"\tilde{P}"}]\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}S"}.

We have,

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S\tilde{P}}=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP}\circ(S\cup\hat{t}_{2})\times(S\cup(\hat{t}_{2}-t_{2})))={\cal V}_{SP}\circ(S\cup\hat{t}_{2})\times(S\cup(\hat{t}_{2}-t_{2})).

By construction according to Algorithm
III
,
\hat{t}_{2},t_{2}
are trees of
{\cal G}_{SP}\circ P,{\cal G}_{SP}\times P,
respectively. Therefore, by Lemma
4
,
\hat{t}_{2},t_{2}
are column bases of

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP}\circ P)={\cal V}_{SP}\circ P
and
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP}\times P)={\cal V}_{SP}\times P,
respectively.

Again according to Algorithm
III
,
\tilde{P}:\equiv\hat{t}_{2}-t_{2}
is a column base of
(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP}\circ P))\circ\hat{t}_{2}\times\tilde{P}={\cal V}_{SP}\circ\hat{t}_{2}\times\tilde{P}

={\cal V}_{SP}\times(P-{t}_{2})\circ\tilde{P}

and
\tilde{P}
is also a column base of
({\cal V}_{SP}\times(P-{t}_{2})\circ\tilde{P})^{\perp}={\cal V}_{SP}^{\perp}\circ(P-{t}_{2})\times\tilde{P}={\cal V}_{SP}^{\perp}\times\hat{t}_{2}\circ\tilde{P}=(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{i}({\cal G}_{SP}\circ P))\times\hat{t}_{2}\circ\tilde{P}.

Let
Q^{1}_{\hat{t}_{2}P}
be a fundamental cutset matrix of
{\cal G}_{SP}\circ P

with respect to
\hat{t}_{2}.

Since
{t}_{2}\subseteq\hat{t}_{2},
we can write

\displaystyle Q^{1}_{\hat{t}_{2}P}=\begin{pmatrix}I_{t_{2}}&0_{t_{2}(\hat{t}_{2}-t_{2})}&K^{1}_{t_{2}(P-\hat{t}_{2})}\\
0_{(\hat{t}_{2}-t_{2})t_{2}}&I_{\hat{t}_{2}-t_{2}}&K_{(\hat{t}_{2}-t_{2})(P-\hat{t}_{2})}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}I_{t_{2}}&0_{t_{2}(\tilde{P})}&K^{1}_{t_{2}(P-\hat{t}_{2})}\\
0_{\tilde{P}t_{2}}&I_{\tilde{P}}&K_{\tilde{P}(P-\hat{t}_{2})}\end{pmatrix}.

(19)

Let
Q^{2}_{\hat{t}_{2}P}
be a fundamental cutset matrix of
{\cal G}_{SP}\times P

with respect to
\hat{t}_{2}.

Since
{t}_{2}\subseteq\hat{t}_{2},
we can write

\displaystyle Q^{2}_{\hat{t}_{2}P}=\begin{pmatrix}I_{t_{2}}&K_{t_{2}(\hat{t}_{2}-t_{2})}&K_{t_{2}(P-\hat{t}_{2})}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}I_{t_{2}}&K_{t_{2}(\tilde{P})}&K_{t_{2}(P-\hat{t}_{2})}\end{pmatrix}.

(20)

We note that
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP}\circ P)=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP})\circ P\supseteq\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP})\times P=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP}\times P).

This means that the rows of
Q^{2}_{\hat{t}_{2}P}
belong to the space
spanned by rows of
Q^{1}_{\hat{t}_{2}P}.

It is also clear that the rows of
Q^{2}_{\hat{t}_{2}P}
together
with the second set of rows of
Q^{1}_{\hat{t}_{2}P}
are linearly
independent, and belong to the space
spanned by rows of
Q^{1}_{\hat{t}_{2}P}.

Further these rows together are the same in number as the rows
of
Q^{1}_{\hat{t}_{2}P}.
We conclude that the matrix
Q_{\hat{t}_{2}P},

shown below, made up of these rows, is a representative matrix of

{\cal V}_{SP}\circ P=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP}\circ P).

\displaystyle Q_{\hat{t}_{2}P}=\begin{pmatrix}I_{t_{2}}&K_{t_{2}(\hat{t}_{2}-t_{2})}&K_{t_{2}(P-\hat{t}_{2})}\\
0_{(\hat{t}_{2}-t_{2})t_{2}}&I_{\hat{t}_{2}-t_{2}}&K_{(\hat{t}_{2}-t_{2})(P-\hat{t}_{2})}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}I_{t_{2}}&K_{t_{2}(\tilde{P})}&K_{t_{2}(P-\hat{t}_{2})}\\
0_{\tilde{P}t_{2}}&I_{\tilde{P}}&K_{\tilde{P}(P-\hat{t}_{2})}\end{pmatrix}.

(21)

Note that, in this representative matrix of
{\cal V}_{SP}\circ P=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP}\circ P),

the space

{\cal V}_{SP}\times P
is visible (the first set of rows forms a
representative matrix of
{\cal V}_{SP}\times P
).
Also visible is the space
{\cal V}_{SP}\circ P\times(P-{t}_{2})
(the second set of rows restricted to columns
(P-{t}_{2}),
forms a
representative matrix of
{\cal V}_{SP}\circ P\times(P-{t}_{2})
)
and the space
{\cal V}_{SP}\circ P\times(P-{t}_{2})\circ\tilde{P}
(the submatrix
I_{\tilde{P}}
in the second set of rows forms a representative matrix of
{\cal V}_{SP}\circ P\times(P-{t}_{2})\circ\tilde{P}
).

We can similarly construct a representative matrix
B_{(P-{t}_{2})P}
of

{\cal V}_{SP}^{\perp}\circ P=\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{i}({\cal G}_{SP}\times P),

in which
the space

{\cal V}_{SP}^{\perp}\times P
is visible.
In this matrix, shown below,
the first set of rows forms a
representative matrix of
{\cal V}_{SP}^{\perp}\times P,

the second set of rows, restricted to
columns
\hat{{t}}_{2},
forms a
representative matrix of

{\cal V}_{SP}^{\perp}\circ P\times\hat{{t}}_{2},

the submatrix
I_{\tilde{P}}
in the second set of rows forms a representative matrix of
{\cal V}_{SP}^{\perp}\circ P\times\hat{{t}}_{2}\circ\tilde{P}={\cal V}_{SP}^{\perp}\circ(P-t_{2})\times\tilde{P}.

\displaystyle B_{(P-{t}_{2})P}=\begin{pmatrix}M_{(P-\hat{t}_{2})t_{2}}&M_{(P-\hat{t}_{2})(\hat{t}_{2}-t_{2})}&I_{P-\hat{t}_{2}}\\
M_{(\hat{t}_{2}-t_{2})t_{2}}&I_{\hat{t}_{2}-t_{2}}&0_{(\hat{t}_{2}-t_{2})(P-\hat{t}_{2})}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}M_{(P-\hat{t}_{2})t_{2}}&M_{(P-\hat{t}_{2})\tilde{P}}&I_{P-\hat{t}_{2}}\\
M_{\tilde{P}t_{2}}&I_{\tilde{P}}&0_{\tilde{P}(P-\hat{t}_{2})}\end{pmatrix}.

(22)

Let
{{\cal A}}_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}\tilde{P}"}:\equiv\alpha_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}\tilde{P}"}+{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}\tilde{P}"}

and let
{{\cal A}}_{{P}^{\prime}{P}"}:\equiv\alpha_{{P}^{\prime}{P}"}+{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{{P}^{\prime}{P}"}.

We will show that
{{\cal A}}_{{P}^{\prime}{P}"}
can be constructed in a simple
way from
{{\cal A}}_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}\tilde{P}"}.

We do this by first relating a solution of
{\cal N}_{P}:\equiv({\cal G}_{SP},{\cal A}_{S^{\prime}S"})
to a solution of
{\cal N}_{\tilde{P}}:\equiv({\cal G}_{S\tilde{P}},{\cal A}_{S^{\prime}S"}).

As before, we denote
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP}),\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{S\tilde{P}}),
respectively by

{\cal V}_{SP},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S\tilde{P}}.

Let
((f_{S^{\prime}},\tilde{f}_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}}),(g_{S"},\tilde{g}_{\tilde{P}"}))
be a solution of
{\cal N}_{\tilde{P}},

i.e.,
((f_{S^{\prime}},\tilde{f}_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}}),({g}_{S"},\tilde{g}_{\tilde{P}"}))\in[(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S\tilde{P}})_{S^{\prime}\tilde{P}^{\prime}}\oplus(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S\tilde{P}}^{\perp})_{S"\tilde{P}"}]\cap{{\cal A}}_{S^{\prime}S"}.

Then the corresponding solutions of
{\cal N}_{P},
i.e., corresponding vectors in

[(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{SP})_{S^{\prime}{P}^{\prime}}\oplus({\cal V}_{SP}^{\perp})_{S"{P}"}]\cap{{\cal A}}_{S^{\prime}S"}

have the form

((f_{S^{\prime}},f_{{P}^{\prime}}),(g_{S"},g_{{P}"})),

where
f_{{P}^{\prime}}\in({\cal V}_{SP}\circ P)_{P^{\prime}},g_{{P}"}\in({\cal V}_{SP}^{\perp}\circ P)_{P"}.

Now the vector
f_{{P}^{\prime}}\in({\cal V}_{SP}\circ P)_{P^{\prime}}
must be a linear combination of the rows of the matrix
Q_{\hat{t}_{2}P}
in Equation
21

and the vector
\tilde{f}_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}}\in{\cal V}_{SP}\circ P\times(P-{t}_{2})\circ\tilde{P}={\cal V}_{SP}\circ\hat{t}_{2}\times\tilde{P}

is a restriction of a linear combination of the second set of rows of

Q_{\hat{t}_{2}P}.
Since this set of rows has an identity matrix corresponding to
columns
\tilde{P},
it follows that

(f_{S^{\prime}},f_{{P}^{\prime}})
can be expanded as

(f_{S^{\prime}},0_{{t^{\prime}}_{2}},\tilde{f}_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}},f_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}}^{T}K_{\tilde{P}(P-\hat{t}_{2})})+(0_{S^{\prime}},h_{{t^{\prime}}_{2}},h_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}},h_{(P-\hat{t}_{2})^{\prime}}),

where

(h_{{t^{\prime}}_{2}},h_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}},h_{(P-\hat{t}_{2})^{\prime}})\in({\cal V}_{SP}\times P)_{P^{\prime}}.

(The first term in the sum corresponds to the second row of
Q_{\hat{t}_{2}P}

and the second term corresponds to the first row of
Q_{\hat{t}_{2}P}

which constitutes a representative matrix of
{\cal V}_{SP}\times P.
)

Next the vector
g_{{P}"}\in({\cal V}_{SP}^{\perp}\circ P)_{P^{\prime}}
must be a linear combination of the rows of the matrix
B_{(P-{t}_{2})P}
in Equation
22

and the vector
\tilde{g}_{\tilde{P}"}\in({\cal V}_{SP}^{\perp}\circ(P-{t}_{2})\times\tilde{P})_{\tilde{P}"},

is a restriction of a linear combination of the second set of rows of

B_{(P-{t}_{2})P}.

We note that this set of rows has an identity matrix corresponding to
columns
\tilde{P}.

Arguing as above,
(g_{S"},\tilde{g}_{\tilde{P}"})
can be expanded as

(g_{S"},g_{\tilde{P}"}^{T}M_{\tilde{P}t_{2}},g_{\tilde{P}"},0_{(P-\hat{t}_{2})"})+(0_{S"},k_{{t"}_{2}},k_{\tilde{P}"},k_{(P-\hat{t}_{2})"}),
where
(k_{{t"}_{2}},k_{\tilde{P}"},k_{(P-\hat{t}_{2})"})\in({\cal V}_{SP}^{\perp}\times P)_{P"}.

Suppose
{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{{P}^{\prime}{P}"},{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}\tilde{P}"}
are the vector space translates of
{{\cal A}}_{{P}^{\prime}{P}"},{{\cal A}}_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}\tilde{P}"},
respectively. The relationship between vectors of
{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{{P}^{\prime}{P}"},{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}\tilde{P}"},
would be the same as the relationship between vectors in
{{\cal A}}_{{P}^{\prime}{P}"},{{\cal A}}_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}\tilde{P}"},
This is so because the same role is played by
{\cal V}_{SP}\circ P,{\cal V}_{SP}\times P,\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S\tilde{P}}\circ\tilde{P},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S\tilde{P}}\times\tilde{P}
and their orthogonal complements in both cases.

Let
({Q}_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}}|{B}_{\tilde{P}"})
be a representative
matrix of
{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}\tilde{P}"}.

Then the matrix
({Q}_{{P}^{\prime}}|{B}_{{P}"}),
shown below, can be a representative
matrix of
{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{{P}^{\prime}{P}"},

taking the columns in the order
(t_{2}^{\prime},\tilde{P}^{\prime},(P-\hat{t}_{2})^{\prime},t"_{2},\tilde{P}",(P-\hat{t}_{2})"),
taking
\tilde{P}:\equiv\hat{t}_{2}-t_{2},

using Equation
21
, Equation
22
,

\displaystyle({Q}_{{P}^{\prime}}|{B}_{{P}"})=\begin{pmatrix}0_{t_{2}^{\prime}}&\ \ {Q}_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}}&\ \ {Q}_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}}(K_{\tilde{P}(P-\hat{t}_{2})})&\vdots\vdots&\ \ {B}_{\tilde{P}"}(M_{\tilde{P}t_{2}})&\ \ {B}_{\tilde{P}"}&\ \ 0_{1(P-\hat{t}_{2})"}\\
I_{t_{2}^{\prime}}&\ \ K_{t_{2}^{\prime}(\tilde{P}^{\prime})}&\ \ K_{t_{2}^{\prime}(P-\hat{t}_{2})^{\prime}}&\vdots\vdots&\ \ 0_{t"_{2}}&\ \ 0_{\tilde{P}"}&\ \ 0_{(P-\hat{t}_{2})"}\\
0_{t_{2}^{\prime}}&\ \ 0_{(\tilde{P}^{\prime})}&\ \ 0_{(P-\hat{t}_{2})^{\prime}}&\vdots\vdots&M_{(P-\hat{t}_{2})"t"_{2}}&\ \ M_{(P-\hat{t}_{2})"\tilde{P}"}&\ \ I_{(P-\hat{t}_{2})"}\end{pmatrix},

(23)

where

M_{\tilde{P}t_{2}}=-K^{T}_{t_{2}\tilde{P}},

K_{\tilde{P}(P-\hat{t}_{2})}=-M^{T}_{(P-\hat{t}_{2})\tilde{P}}\ ,

using the orthogonality of
{\cal V}_{SP}\times P,{\cal V}_{SP}^{\perp}\circ P

and

{\cal V}_{SP}\circ P,{\cal V}_{SP}^{\perp}\times P.

D.2
Testing independence of columns of a multiport behaviour using its port minimization

Problem 81
.

Let
P_{1}^{\prime}
be a set of columns of a multiport behaviour
{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}.

1.

Test whether
P_{1}^{\prime}
is an independent set of columns of
{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}\circ P^{\prime}
and of
{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}\times P^{\prime}.

2.

Test whether
P_{1}"
is an independent set of columns of
{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}\circ P"
and of
{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}\times P".

There are situations where a multiport behaviour
{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}:\equiv[(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP}))_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}}\oplus(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{i}({\cal G}_{SP})_{S"P"}]\leftrightarrow{\cal A}_{S^{\prime}S"}
has size of
P
much greater than that of
S

and we have to test independence as in Problem
81

.
We have seen that the minimized version
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}\tilde{P}"}

of
{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}
(using Algorithm
III
)
has
|\tilde{P}|\leq|S|.

It is therefore worthwhile inferring the independence of columns
P_{1}^{\prime}\subseteq P^{\prime}
of
{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}\circ P^{\prime},{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}\times P^{\prime}

by computing the independence of columns
(P_{1}\cap\tilde{P})^{\prime}

of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}\tilde{P}"}\circ\tilde{P}^{\prime},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}\tilde{P}"}\times\tilde{P}^{\prime}.

A similar exercise is worth doing with the
"
entities.

Let the columns
P_{1}^{\prime}

of
(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{SP})_{S^{\prime}{P}^{\prime}}
be dependent. Then some nonzero vector
k_{P_{1}^{\prime}}
on
P_{1}^{\prime}
is orthogonal
to the columns
P_{1}^{\prime}
of
(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{SP})_{S^{\prime}{P}^{\prime}}.

Suppose
((f_{S^{\prime}},f_{{P}_{1}^{\prime}},f_{({P}-P_{1})^{\prime}}),(g_{S"},g_{{P}_{1}"},g_{({P}-P_{1})"}))
is a vector in

[(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{SP})_{S^{\prime}{P}^{\prime}}\oplus({\cal V}_{SP}^{\perp})_{S"{P}"}]\cap{{\cal A}}_{S^{\prime}S"}.

It is clear that
k_{P_{1}^{\prime}}

will also be
orthogonal to the vector
f_{{P}_{1}^{\prime}}.

Since
{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}:\equiv([(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{SP})_{S^{\prime}{P}^{\prime}}\oplus({\cal V}_{SP}^{\perp})_{S"\tilde{P}"}]\cap{{\cal A}}_{S^{\prime}S"})\circ P^{\prime}P",
it follows that the columns
P_{1}^{\prime}

of
{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}
must
also be dependent.
We conclude that if a set of columns
P_{1}^{\prime}

of
{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}
is to be independent, it is necessary that these
columns of
(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{SP})_{S^{\prime}{P}^{\prime}},
also be independent.

Let columns
P_{1}^{\prime}
of
(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{SP})_{S^{\prime}{P}^{\prime}},
be independent.
By Lemma
4
,
P_{1}
is a subset of a tree of

{\cal G}_{SP}\circ P.
Therefore, in Algorithm
III
,
we may take
P_{1}\subseteq\hat{t}_{2}.
(We may additionally take
t_{2}
to contain as many edges
of
P_{1}
as possible - this is not necessary, but will save effort later.) From Equation
23
, it is clear
that a subset
P_{1}^{\prime}
of columns of
\tilde{P}^{\prime}\cup t_{2}^{\prime}=\hat{t}_{2}^{\prime}
is independent
in the representative matrix
({Q}_{{P}^{\prime}}|{B}_{{P}"})
of

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{P^{\prime}P"}
iff
P_{1}^{\prime}\cap\tilde{P}^{\prime}
is independent in the representative
matrix
({Q}_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}}|{B}_{\tilde{P}"})
of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{\tilde{P^{\prime}}\tilde{P"}}.

We note that
P_{1}^{\prime}
is an independent subset of columns of
{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}
iff it is an independent
subset of columns of
{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}\circ P^{\prime}.

Next we can derive a representative matrix for
{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}\cap 0_{P"}

from Equation
23
, as shown below.

\displaystyle(\hat{Q}_{{P}^{\prime}}|0_{{P}"})=\begin{pmatrix}0_{1t_{2}^{\prime}}&\ \ \hat{Q}_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}}&\ \ \hat{Q}_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}}(K_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}(P-\hat{t}_{2})^{\prime}})&\vdots\vdots&\ \ 0_{2t"_{2}}&\ \ 0_{\tilde{P}"}&\ \ 0_{1(P-\hat{t}_{2})"}\\
I_{t_{2}^{\prime}}&\ \ K_{t_{2}^{\prime}(\tilde{P}^{\prime})}&\ \ K_{t_{2}^{\prime}(P-\hat{t}_{2})^{\prime}}&\vdots\vdots&\ \ 0_{2t"_{2}}&\ \ 0_{\tilde{P}"}&\ \ 0_{1(P-\hat{t}_{2})"}\end{pmatrix},

(24)

where the first set of rows is a suitable linear combination of the
first set of rows of
({Q}_{{P}^{\prime}}|{B}_{{P}"})
in Equation
23
.
It is clear once again
that a subset
P_{1}^{\prime}
of columns of
\tilde{P}^{\prime}\cup t_{2}^{\prime}=\hat{t}_{2}^{\prime}
is independent
in the representative matrix
(\hat{Q}_{{P}^{\prime}}|0_{{P}"})
of

{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{\tilde{P^{\prime}}\tilde{P"}}\cap 0_{P"}
iff
P_{1}^{\prime}\cap\tilde{P}^{\prime}
is independent in the representative
matrix
(\hat{Q}_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}}|0_{\tilde{P}"})
of
{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{\tilde{P^{\prime}}\tilde{P"}}\cap 0_{P"}.

It follows that a subset
P_{1}^{\prime}
of columns of
\tilde{P}^{\prime}\cup t_{2}^{\prime}=\hat{t}_{2}^{\prime}
is independent
in the representative matrix
(\hat{Q}_{{P}^{\prime}})
of

{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}\times{P^{\prime}}
iff
P_{1}^{\prime}\cap\tilde{P}^{\prime}
is independent in the representative
matrix
(\hat{Q}_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}})
of
{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{\tilde{P^{\prime}}\tilde{P"}}\times\tilde{P}^{\prime}.

The argument for the independence of columns
P_{1}"
is similar.
If a set of columns
P_{1}"

of
{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}
is to be independent, it is necessary that these
columns of
(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{SP}^{\perp})_{S"\tilde{P}"},
also be independent.
Let columns
P_{1}"
of
(\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{SP})^{\perp}_{S"\tilde{P}"},
be independent.
By Lemma
4
,
P_{1}
is a subset of a cotree of

{\cal G}_{SP}\times P,
which may be taken to be
P-t_{2}.
Now
P-\hat{t}_{2}\subseteq P-t_{2}.

We may take
P-\hat{t}_{2}
to contain as many edges of
P_{1}
as possible
so that
(P-t_{2})-(P-\hat{t}_{2})=\hat{t}_{2}-t_{2}=\tilde{P}
contains as few
edges of
P_{1}
as possible (this is not necessary, but will save effort later).
From Equation
23
, it is clear
that a subset
P_{1}"
of columns of
\tilde{P}"\cup(P-\hat{t}_{2})"=(P-{t}_{2})"
is independent
in the representative matrix
({Q}_{{P}^{\prime}}|{B}_{{P}"})
of

\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{P^{\prime}P"}
iff
P_{1}"\cap\tilde{P}"
is independent in the representative
matrix
({Q}_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}}|{B}_{\tilde{P}"})
of
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{\tilde{P^{\prime}}\tilde{P"}}.

The proof that a subset
P_{1}"
of columns of
{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}\times P"

is independent iff
P_{1}"\cap\tilde{P}"
is an independent subset of columns
of
{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}\times P"
is similar to the
P_{1}^{\prime}
case for

{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}\times P^{\prime}.

Remark 13
.

{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}\times P^{\prime},{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}\times P"
are respectively the open circuit and short circuit port behaviours of
{\cal N}_{P}.
Even if
{\cal N}_{P}
is rigid,
they cannot be computed directly using conventional circuit simulators, by open circuiting or short circuiting the ports and computing the solution of the resulting network. This is because,
in general this network has non unique solution so cannot be handled
by conventional circuit simulators.

We summarize the above discussion in the form of an algorithm
for testing independence of subsets of columns of

{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}\circ P^{\prime},{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}\times P^{\prime},{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}\circ P",{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}\times P".

Algorithm IV

Input: A linear multiport
{\cal N}_{P}:\equiv({\cal G}_{SP},\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}S"}),
and a subset
P_{1}\subseteq P.

Output: A statement whether
P_{1}^{\prime}
is independent in
{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}\circ P^{\prime},{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}\times P^{\prime},

and whether
P_{1}"
is independent in
{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}\circ P",{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}\times P",
where

{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}:\equiv\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{v}({\cal G}_{SP})_{S^{\prime}P^{\prime}}\oplus\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}^{i}({\cal G}_{SP})_{S"P"}\leftrightarrow\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{S^{\prime}S"}.

1.

(
P_{1}^{\prime}
) Step 1. Check if
P_{1}
is loop free in
{\cal G}_{SP}.
If not,
declare that
P_{1}^{\prime}
is

not independent in
{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}\circ P^{\prime},

and not independent in
{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}\times P^{\prime}.

If yes, construct minimal
{\cal N}_{\tilde{P}}
as in Algorithm
III
, taking
\hat{t}_{2}\supseteq P_{1},\tilde{P}:\equiv\hat{t}_{2}-{t}_{2}
and taking
t_{2}\cap P_{1}
as large as possible.

Step 2. Compute a representative matrix
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}\tilde{P}"}
as in Algorithm
I
and, using it,

a representative matrix for
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}\tilde{P}"}\cap{\mathbf{0}}_{P"}.

Step 3. Check if the subset of columns
(P_{1}\cap\tilde{P})^{\prime}
is independent in the above two

representative matrices. If it is independent (dependent) declare

P_{1}^{\prime}
to be an independent (dependent) set of columns in the corresponding vector space,
but replacing
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}\tilde{P}"}\cap{\mathbf{0}}_{P"}

by
{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}\times P^{\prime}.

2.

(
P_{1}"
)
Step 1. Check if
P_{1}
is cutset free in
{\cal G}_{SP},
i.e., whether
(S\cup P)-P_{1}
contains a tree of
{\cal G}_{SP}.
If not,
declare that
P_{1}"
is

not independent in
{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}\circ P",

and not independent in
{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}\times P".

If yes, construct minimal
{\cal N}_{\tilde{P}}
as in Algorithm
III
, taking
(P-{t}_{2})\supseteq P_{1}
and taking

(P-\hat{t}_{2})\cap P_{1}
as large as possible.

Step 2. Compute a representative matrix
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}\tilde{P}"}
as in Algorithm
I
and, using it,

a representative matrix for
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}\tilde{P}"}\cap{\mathbf{0}}_{P^{\prime}}.

Step 3. Check if the subset of columns
(P_{1}\cap\tilde{P})"
is independent in the above two

representative matrices. If it is independent (dependent) declare

P_{1}"
to be an independent (dependent) set of columns in the corresponding vector space,
but replacing
\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}_{\tilde{P}^{\prime}\tilde{P}"}\cap{\mathbf{0}}_{P^{\prime}}

by
{\mbox{$\mathcal{V}$}}_{P^{\prime}P"}\times P".

STOP

Remark 14
.

In Algorithm
IV
, while testing for independence
of
P_{1}^{\prime},
we have taken
P_{1}\cap t_{2}
as large as possible, so that

P_{1}\cap(\hat{t}_{2}-t_{2})
is as small as possible.
This can be done as follows.
Let
t_{1}
be a tree of
{\cal G}_{SP}\circ S.
Grow this into a tree
t
of
{\cal G}_{SP}

by using as many edges of
P_{1}
as possible. Take
t_{2}:\equiv t-t_{1}.

Grow this into a tree
\hat{t}_{2}
of
{\cal G}_{SP}\circ P,

using edges of
P_{1}
to begin with. Since
P_{1}
is loop free in
{\cal G}_{SP},

we must have
P_{1}\subseteq\hat{t}_{2}.

Further
|P_{1}\cap(\hat{t}_{2}-t_{2})|
is a minimum.

While testing for independence
of
P_{1}",
we have taken
P_{1}\cap(P-\hat{t}_{2})
as large as possible, so that

P_{1}\cap((P-t_{2})-(P-\hat{t}_{2}))=P_{1}\cap(\hat{t}_{2}-t_{2})
is as small as possible.
This can be done as follows.
Grow a tree
\hat{t}_{2}
of
{\cal G}_{SP}\circ P
that contains as few edges of
P_{1}

as possible. Let
t_{1}
be a tree of
{\cal G}_{SP}\circ S.
Grow this into a tree
t
of
{\cal G}_{SP}
using edges of
\hat{t}_{2}-P_{1}
first.
Let
t_{2}:\equiv t\cap\hat{t}_{2}.

Since
P_{1}
is cutset free in
{\cal G}_{SP},

we must have
P_{1}\subseteq P-{t}_{2}.

Further
|P_{1}\cap(\hat{t}_{2}-t_{2})|
is a minimum.
